At that her nerves suddenly got the better of her and her mind went mad.… Supposing that that fellow, from whom she had not heard for two years, *hadn’t* now communicated with her.… Like an ass she had taken it for granted that he had *asked* Lady… Blast her!… to “bring them together again”! She had imagined that even Edith Ethel would not have had the cheek to ring her up if he hadn’t asked her to!

But she had nothing to go on.… Feeble, oversexed ass that she was, she had let her mind jump at once to the conclusion, the moment the mere mention of him seemed implied—jump to the conclusion that he was asking her again to come and be his mistress.… Or nurse him through his present muddle till he should be fit to.…

Mind, she did not say that she would have succumbed. But if she had not jumped at the idea that it was he, really, speaking through Edith Ethel, she would never have permitted her mind to dwell on… on his blasted, complacent perfections!

Because she had taken it for granted that if he had had her rung up he would not have been monkeying with other girls during the two years he hadn’t written to her.… Ah, but hadn’t he?
===Chinese===
想到这里，她的神经突然占了上风，脑子发了疯。……万一那个两年来杳无音信的家伙，这会儿压根*没有*联络她呢。……她像头蠢驴似的想当然，以为是他*央求了*那位夫……布莱……去她的！……让她来「再牵线重归于好」！她还以为，若不是他开了口，连伊迪丝·埃塞尔也没这份脸皮打这个电话！

可她毫无凭据。……她这头欲火中烧的软蛋蠢驴，话里才刚仿佛影到他的名字，就让脑子一个箭步跳到了结论上——跳到「他又在请她去当他的情妇」的结论上。……或者去护理他熬过眼下这摊烂事，等他缓过劲来好……

请注意，她并不是说她就会就范。可要不是她一口认定，那真是他借伊迪丝·埃塞尔之口在说话，她绝不会容自己的脑子流连于……流连于他那些该死的、心安理得的十全十美！

因为她想当然：既然他让人给她打了电话，那么不曾给她写信的那两年里，他总不至于跟别的姑娘厮混。……啊，是吗？他真没有吗？

===Original===
Look here! *Was* it reasonable? Here was a fellow who had all but… all **but**… “taken advantage of her” one night just before going out to France, say, two years ago.… And not another word from him after that!… It was all very well to say that he was portentous, looming, luminous, loony: John Peel with his coat so grey, the English Country Gentleman *pur sang* and then some; saintly; Godlike, Jesus-Christ-like.… He was all that. But you don’t seduce, as near as can be, a young woman and then go off to Hell, leaving her, God knows, in Hell, and not so much as send her, in two years, a picture-postcard with **mizpah** on it. You don’t. You don’t!

Or if you do you have to have your character revised. You have to have it taken for granted that you were only monkeying with her and that you’ve been monkeying ever since with WAACS in Rouen or some other Base.…

Of course, if you ring your young woman up when you come back… or have her rung up by a titled lady.… That might restore you in the eyes of the world, or at least in the eyes of the young woman if she was a bit of a softie.…

But *had* he? *Had* he? It was absurd to think that Edith Ethel hadn’t had the face to do it unasked! To save three thousand two hundred pounds, not to mention interest—which was what Vincent owed *him*!—Edith Ethel with the sweetest possible smile would beg the pillows off a whole hospital ward full of dying.… She was quite right. She had to save her man. You go to any depths of ignominy to save your man.
===Chinese===
你听好！这*讲得*通吗？有这么一位老兄，已经差不多……已经**就差一点**……「占了她便宜」，就在开拔去法国的前一夜，比如说，两年前。……此后便再无只言片语！……尽可以说他奇伟骇人、庞然逼人、莹莹生辉、疯疯癫癫：像歌谣里那位灰外套的约翰·皮尔（John Peel），*纯血*（pur sang）的英国乡村绅士还犹有过之；圣徒一般；神一般，耶稣基督一般。……他全都是。可你不能差不多等于诱奸了一个年轻姑娘，然后自己动身下地狱，把她——天知道——撇在地狱里，两年之中，连一张印着**米兹帕**（mizpah）的美术明信片都不肯寄。你不能。你不能！

你要是真这么干了，你的品行就得重新核定。人家就会想当然：你不过跟她闹着玩，而且打那以后，一直在鲁昂或别的什么后方基地，跟陆军妇女辅助队（WAACS）的姑娘们闹着玩。……

当然，要是你回来时给年轻姑娘打个电话……或者请一位有头衔的夫人代打。……这兴许能让你在世人眼里挽回名誉，或者至少在那位姑娘眼里——如果她有点软心肠的话。……

可他*求过*吗？他*求过*吗？若以为伊迪丝·埃塞尔非得受人之托才拉得下这个脸，那才荒唐！为了省下三千二百镑——且不算利息——那可是文森特欠*他*的！——伊迪丝·埃塞尔肯带上天下最甜的笑脸，去向满满一病房的垂死病人讨枕头。……她做得对。她得救她的男人。为救自己的男人，多深的屈辱都下得去。

===Original===
But that did not help her, Valentine Wannop!

She sprang off the bench; she clenched her nails into her palms; she stamped her thin-soled shoes into the coke-brise floor that was singularly unresilient. She exclaimed:

“Damn it all, he didn’t ask her to ring me up. He didn’t ask her to. He didn’t ask her to!” still stamping about.

She marched straight at the telephone that was by now uttering long, tinny, nightjar’s calls and, with one snap, pulled the receiver right off the twisted, green-blue cord.… Broke it! With incidental satisfaction.

Then she said:

“Steady the Buffs!” not out of repentance for having damaged School Property, but because she was accustomed to call her thoughts The Buffs because of their practical, unromantic character as a rule.… A fine regiment, the Buffs!
===Chinese===
可这帮不了她瓦伦丁·万诺普什么忙！

她从长凳上一跃而起；指甲掐进掌心；薄底鞋狠狠跺在毫无弹性的焦渣地上。她嚷道：

「见鬼，他没求她给我打电话。他没求她。他就是没求她！」一边还跺着脚打转。

她大步直冲那台此时正发出长而尖细的夜鹰叫的电话，咔嚓一声，把听筒从那根拧成麻花的蓝绿花线上整个拽了下来。……扯坏了！顺手之快，颇为受用。

然后她说：

「巴夫斯团，稳住！」（Steady the Buffs!）倒不是为损坏了校产而悔恨，而是因为她习惯把自己的念头唤作巴夫斯团，因为它们照例讲求实际、毫不浪漫。……好一个团队，巴夫斯团！

===Original===
Of course, if she had not broken the telephone she could have rung up Edith Ethel and have asked her whether he had or hadn’t asked to… to be brought together again.… It was like her, Valentine Wannop, to smash the only means of resolving a torturing doubt.…

It wasn’t, really, in the least like her. *She* was practical enough: none of the “under the ban of fatality” business about her. She had smashed the telephone because it had been like smashing a connection with Edith Ethel; or because she hated tinny nightjars; or because she had smashed it. For nothing in the world; for nothing, nothing, nothing in the world would she ever ring up Edith Ethel and ask her:

“Did *he* put you up to ringing me up?”

That would be to let Edith Ethel come between their intimacy.

A subconscious volition was directing her feet towards the great doors at the end of the Hall, varnished, pitch-pine doors of Gothic architecture; economically decorated as if with straps and tin-lids of Brunswick-blacked cast iron.
===Chinese===
当然，要是她没砸坏电话，她本可以打给伊迪丝·埃塞尔，问问她他到底求没求过……求没求过「重归于好」。……她瓦伦丁·万诺普就是这个脾气，专会砸掉唯一能解开钻心疑团的工具。……

说真的，这半点也不像她。*她*够讲求实际的：她身上没有一丝「命定厄运笼罩」的调调。她砸了电话，是因为砸电话就像砸断与伊迪丝·埃塞尔的一线牵连；或因为她恨尖声叫唤的夜鹰；或因为砸都砸了。给什么都不干；给天底下的什么、什么、什么都不换——她绝不会打给伊迪丝·埃塞尔去问：

「是他*撺掇*你给我打的电话吗？」

那就等于让伊迪丝·埃塞尔插进他们两人的私交中间。

一股潜意识的意志正引导她的双脚，走向礼堂尽头的大门——上过清漆的脂松木哥特式大门；装饰得极为俭省，仿佛缀着涂不伦瑞克黑漆的铸铁皮带扣和铁皮盖。

===Original===
She said:

“Of course if it’s his wife who has removed his furniture that would be a reason for his wanting to get into communication. They would have split.… But he does not hold with a man divorcing a woman, and she won’t divorce.”

As she went through the sticky postern—All that woodwork seemed sticky on account of its varnish!—beside the great doors she said:

“Who cares!”

The great thing was… but she could not formulate what the great thing was. You had to settle the preliminaries.
===Chinese===
她说：

「当然，如果是他太太搬走了他的家具，那他想恢复联络，倒还情有可原。他们多半掰了。……可他不赞成男人休妻，而她又不肯离婚。」

她穿过那道黏糊糊的边门时——那些木作因为上了清漆，仿佛全都黏糊糊的！——就在大门旁边，她说：

「谁在乎！」

要紧的是……可她说不出要紧的究竟是什么。总得先把前提理清楚。

### III / 第三章

===Original===
She said eventually to Miss Wanostrocht who had sat down at her table behind two pink carnations:

“I didn’t consciously want to bother you but a spirit in my feet has led me who knows how.… That’s Shelley, isn’t it?”

And indeed a quite unconscious but shrewd mind had pointed out to her whilst still in the School Hall and even before she had broken the telephone, that Miss Wanostrocht very probably would be able to tell her what she wanted to know and that if she didn’t hurry she might miss her, since the Head would probably go now the girls were gone. So she had hurried through gauntish corridors whose Decorated Gothic windows positively had bits of pink glass here and there interspersed in their lattices. Nevertheless a nearly deserted, darkish, locker-lined dressing-room being a shortcut, she had paused in it before the figure of a clumsyish girl, freckled, in black and, on a stool, desultorily lacing a dull black boot, an ankle on her knee. She felt an impulse to say: “Goodbye, Pettigul!” she didn’t know why.

The clumsy, fifteenish, bumpy-faced girl was a symbol of that place—healthyish, but not over healthy; honestish but with no craving for intellectual honesty; big-boned in unexpected places… and uncomelily blubbering so that her face appeared dirtyish.… It was in fact all “ishes” about that Institution. They were all healthyish, honestish, clumsyish, twelve-to-eighteenish and big-boned in unexpected places because of the late insufficient feeding.… Emotionalish, too; apt to blubber rather than to go into hysterics.

===Chinese===
她终于对万诺斯特拉奇小姐开了口——后者已在自己的桌子后落了座，桌前摆着两枝粉色康乃馨：

「我并非存心要打扰您，可我脚底下有个精灵，不知怎的就把我领到这儿来了。……这是雪莱的诗句，对不对？」

确实，早在学校礼堂里、甚至在她摔坏电话机之前，她心里那个全然无意却精明不过的头脑就已向她指出：万诺斯特拉奇小姐多半能告诉她她想打听的事；而且她若不赶紧些，没准就扑空了，因为女学生既已散去，校长多半也要走了。于是她匆匆穿过那些颇为空旷的走廊——走廊里装饰派哥特式（Decorated Gothic）的花窗上，格条之间当真零零星星嵌着几块粉色玻璃。话虽如此，一间几近无人、光线幽暗、四壁排满储物柜的更衣室既然是条近路，她便在其中停了停，停在一个笨手笨脚的女孩子面前——那孩子满脸雀斑，一身黑衣，坐在小凳上，一脚脚踝搁在膝头，心不在焉地系着一只乌沉沉的黑靴子。她心头涌起一股冲动，想道一声：「再见，佩蒂古尔！」她说不清为什么。

这个笨拙的、十五六岁、疙瘩脸的女孩，正是那个地方的象征——还算健康，却算不上十分健康；还算诚实，却绝不渴求智识上的诚实；骨架子大得不是地方……此刻正不成体统地抽噎着，一张脸哭得显出脏兮兮的样子来。……事实上，这所学堂里处处都是这路「还算」。人人都还算健康、还算诚实、还算笨拙、十二到十八岁上下，又因为近来伙食不足，骨架子大得不是地方。……也还算多愁善感；动辄抽抽搭搭，倒还不至于闹到歇斯底里。

===Original===
Instead of saying goodbye to the girl she said:

“Here!” and roughly, since she was exhibiting too much leg, pulled down the girl’s shortish skirt and set to work to lace the unyielding boot on the unyielding shinbone.… After a period of youthful bloom, which would certainly come and as certainly go, this girl would, normally, find herself one of the Mothers of Europe, marriage being due to the period of youthful bloom.… Normally that is to say according to a normality that that day might restore. Of course it mightn’t!

A tepid drop of moisture fell on Valentine’s right knuckle.

“My cousin Bob was killed the day before yesterday,” the girl’s voice said above her head. Valentine bent her head still lower over the boot with the patience that, in educational establishments, you must, if you want to be businesslike and shrewd, acquire and display in face of unusual mental vagaries.… This girl had never had a cousin Bob, or anything else. Pettigul and her two sisters, Pettiguls Two and Three, were all in that Institution at extremely reduced rates precisely because they had not got, apart from their widowed mother, a discoverable relative. The father, a half-pay major, had been killed early in the war. All the mistresses had had to hand in reports on the moral qualities of the Pettiguls, so all the mistresses had this information.

===Chinese===
她没有向那孩子道再见，而是说：

「来！」随即——因为那孩子露出的腿实在太多——粗手粗脚地把她过短的裙子往下拽了拽，然后动手去系那只不肯服帖的靴子，靴筒里是一根同样不肯服帖的胫骨。……待到一段青春韶华——这段韶华必定会来，也必定会走——这个女孩照理会发现自己成了欧洲的母亲们中间的一员，因为婚姻正是青春韶华该结出的果子。……所谓照理，是照着那一天或许会恢复的正常秩序说的。当然，也可能恢复不了！

一滴温吞吞的泪水落在瓦伦丁右手的中指关节上。

「我表哥鲍勃前天阵亡了，」女孩的声音在她头顶上方响起。瓦伦丁把头对着靴子埋得更低了，带着那份在教育机构里你必须练就、也必须摆出的耐心——倘若你想行事干练、头脑精明的话——来面对种种反常的心智怪态。……这女孩压根没有什么表哥鲍勃，别的什么亲戚也没有。佩蒂古尔和她的两个妹妹——佩蒂古尔老二和老三——之所以能以极低的学费待在这所学堂里，恰恰因为她们除了寡居的母亲之外，再没有一个查得出来的亲戚。她们的父亲，一个领半薪的少校，战争初期就阵亡了。所有女教员都得递交关于佩蒂古尔姐妹品行操守的报告，所以所有女教员都掌握这一情况。

===Original===
“He gave me his puppy to keep for him before he went out,” the girl said. “It doesn’t seem just!”

Valentine, straightening herself, said:

“I should wash my face if I were you, before I went out. Or you might get yourself taken for a German!” She pulled the girl’s clumsyish blouse straight on her shoulders.

“Try,” she added, “to imagine that you’ve got someone just come back! It’s just as easy and it will make you look more attractive!”

Scurrying along the corridors she said to herself:

“Heaven help me, does it make *me* look more attractive?”

===Chinese===
「他出征之前，把他的小狗留给我替他照看，」女孩说。「这也太不像话了！」

瓦伦丁直起身子，说：

「我要是你，出门之前就把脸洗一洗。不然人家没准把你当成德国人！」她把那女孩肩上皱作一团的笨拙罩衫拉平了。

「试试看，」她补了一句，「就当是有个人刚刚回家来了！这一样容易，还能让你显得更好看！」

她一路小跑着穿过走廊，心里自问：

「老天爷帮帮忙，这一招能让*我*显得更好看吗？」

---

===Original===
She caught the Head, as she had anticipated, just on the point of going to her home in Fulham, an unattractive suburb but near a bishop’s palace nevertheless. It seemed somehow appropriate. The lady was episcopally-minded but experienced in the vicissitudes of suburban children: very astonishing some of them unless you took them very much in the lump.

Miss Head had stood behind her table for the first three questions and answers, in an attitude of someone who is a little at bay, but she had sat down just before Valentine had quoted her Shelley at her, and she had now the air of one who is ready to make a night of it. Valentine continued to stand.

===Chinese===
不出她所料，她赶上了正要动身回富勒姆（Fulham）家去的校长——那是个不起眼的郊区，可偏偏挨着一座主教宅邸。不知怎么，这显得挺相称。这位女士满脑子主教气，然而对郊区孩子的种种境遇变迁阅历颇深：其中有些孩子着实惊人，除非你把他们整个儿囫囵着看。

头三个来回的问答，校长一直在自己桌子后头站着，一副略略被逼到墙角的人的姿态；可就在瓦伦丁对她引出雪莱诗句之前，她坐了下来，如今摆出一副准备奉陪通宵的架势。瓦伦丁则始终站着。

===Original===
“This,” Miss Wanostrocht said very gently, “is a day on which one might… take steps… that might influence one’s whole life.”

“That’s,” Valentine answered, “exactly why I’ve come to you. I want to know what that woman said to you so as to know where I stand before I take a step.”

The Head said:

“I had to let the girls go. I don’t mind saying that you are very valuable to me. The Governors—I had an express from Lord Boulnois—ordered them to be given a holiday tomorrow. It’s very inconsistent. But that makes it all the.…”

She stopped. Valentine said to herself:

“By Jove, I don’t know anything about men; but how little I know about women. What’s she getting at?”

She added:

“She’s nervous. She must be wanting to say something she thinks I won’t like!”

===Chinese===
「今天，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐温温和和地说，「是个可以……迈出几步……说不定影响一生的日子。」

「这正是，」瓦伦丁答道，「我为什么来找您。我想知道那个女人都对您说了些什么，好弄清自己的处境，再决定迈不迈这一步。」

校长说：

「我不得不放女学生们回家去了。我不妨直说，你对我非常宝贵。校董们——布尔努瓦勋爵（Lord Boulnois）给我来了封快信——下令明天给她们放假。这很自相矛盾。可这样一来，事情就全……」

她停住了。瓦伦丁心想：

「天哪，男人的事我一概不知；可女人的事我又知道得何其少。她到底想说什么？」

她又想：

「她是紧张了。她一定有句什么话想说，又认定我不会爱听！」

===Original===
She said chivalrously:

“I don’t believe anybody could have kept those girls in today. It’s a thing one has no experience of. There’s never been a day like this before.”

Out there in Piccadilly there would be seething mobs shoulder to shoulder: she had never seen the Nelson column stand out of a solid mass. They might roast oxen whole in the Strand: Whitechapel would be seething, enamelled iron advertisements looking down on millions of bowler hats. All sordid and immense London stretched out under her gaze. She felt herself *of* London as the grouse feels itself of the heather, and there she was in an emptied suburb looking at two pink carnations. Dyed probably: offering of Lord Boulnois to Miss Wanostrocht! You never saw a natural-grown carnation that shade!

She said:

“I’d be glad to know what that woman—Lady Macmaster—told you.”

===Chinese===
于是她仗义地说：

「我不信今天有谁能让那些女孩子安安分分留在学校里。这种事谁都没有经验。从来没有过这样的日子。」

此刻在皮卡迪利大街上，该是人山人海、肩挨着肩地沸腾了：她还从没见过纳尔逊纪念柱从一整块实心的人堆里拔身而出的景象。人们没准会在斯特兰德大街上烤整头的公牛：白教堂区也会沸腾起来，搪瓷铁皮的广告牌俯瞰着几百万顶圆顶礼帽。整个肮脏而宏大的伦敦，在她凝望之下铺展开去。她感到自己*属于*伦敦，正如松鸡感到自己属于石楠荒原，可她此刻却在一个人去楼空的郊区，望着一对粉色康乃馨。多半是染的：布尔努瓦勋爵献给万诺斯特拉奇小姐的贡品！天然长成的康乃馨，你可从没见过那种颜色！

她说：

「我倒很乐意知道那个女人——麦克马斯特夫人——都对您说了些什么。」

===Original===
Miss Wanostrocht looked down at her hands. She had the little-fingers hooked together, the hands back to back; it was a demoded gesture.… Girton of 1897, Valentine thought. Indulged in by the thoughtfully blonde.… Fair girl graduates the sympathetic comic papers of those days had called them. It pointed to a long sitting. Well, she, Valentine, was not going to brusque the issue!… French-derived expression that. But how would you put it otherwise?

Miss Wanostrocht said:

“I sat at the feet of your father!”

“You see!” Valentine said to herself. “But she must then have gone to Oxford, not Newnham!” She could not remember whether there had been woman’s colleges at Oxford as early as 1895 or 1897. There must have been.

“The greatest Teacher.… The greatest influence in the world,” Miss Wanostrocht said.

It was queer, Valentine thought: This woman had known all about her—at any rate all about her distinguished descent all the time she, Valentine, had been Physical Instructress at that Great Public School (Girls’). Yet except for an invariable courtesy such as she imagined Generals might show to noncommissioned officers, Miss Wanostrocht had hitherto taken no more notice of her than she might have taken of a superior parlourmaid. On the other hand she had let Valentine arrange her physical training exactly as she liked: without any interference.

===Chinese===
万诺斯特拉奇小姐低头看了看自己的双手。她两只小指互相勾着，手背对手背；这是个过了时的姿势。……1897 年的格顿女才子，瓦伦丁想。专属于那些金发而若有所思的女子。……当年那些爱凑趣的滑稽报刊，管她们叫「金发女毕业生」。这预示着一场长谈。好吧，她瓦伦丁可不打算把话逼到摊牌！……这是个法语来的说法。可不然你又会怎么说呢？

万诺斯特拉奇小姐说：

「我曾在令尊膝下受教！」

「这就对了！」瓦伦丁心想。「那她当年上的必定是牛津，不是纽纳姆（Newnham）！」她记不清牛津是否早在 1895 年或 1897 年就已有女子学院。想必是有的。

「最伟大的导师。……世上最有影响的人物，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐说。

真怪，瓦伦丁想：这个女人对她的底细一清二楚——至少对她那显赫的家世一清二楚——在她瓦伦丁于那所伟大的公学（女子部）担任体育教员的整个期间，向来如此。然而，除了那种一成不变的客气——就像她想象中将军们对军士们会表示的那种——万诺斯特拉奇小姐迄今对她的留意，并不比对一个领班的客厅女仆更多。可在另一方面，她又任由瓦伦丁随心所欲地安排体育教学：从不横加干涉。

===Original===
“We used to hear,” Miss Wanostrocht said, “how he spoke Latin with you and your brother from the day of your births.… He used to be regarded as eccentric, but how *right*!… Miss Hall says that you are the most remarkable Latinist she has ever so much as imagined.”

“It’s not true,” Valentine said, “I can’t *think* in Latin. You cannot be a real Latinist unless you do that. He did of course.”

“It was the last thing you would think of him as doing,” the Head answered with a pale gleam of youth. “He was such a thorough man of the world. So awake!”

===Chinese===
「我们从前常听说，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐说，「他如何从你们兄妹俩出生那天起，就跟你们说拉丁文。……他从前被目为怪人，可他是多么*正确*啊！……霍尔小姐（Miss Hall）说，你是她连想象都不曾想象过的最了不起的拉丁文大家。」

「这不是真的，」瓦伦丁说，「我没法用拉丁文*思考*。做不到这一点，就算不上真正的拉丁文大家。他当然是做得到的。」

「谁也想不到他竟会做这样的事，」校长答道，苍白的脸上闪过一丝青春的光彩。「他是那样一个彻头彻尾的老于世故的人。那样地醒豁！」

===Original===
“We ought to be a queer lot, my brother and I,” Valentine said. “With such a father… And mother of course!”

Miss Wanostrocht said:

“Oh… your *mother*.…”

And immediately Valentine conjured up the little, adoring female clique of Miss Wanostrocht’s youth, all spying on her father and mother in their walks under the Oxford Sunday trees, the father so jaunty and awake, the mother so trailing, large, generous, unobservant. And all the little clique saying: If only he had *us* to look after him.… She said with a little malice:

“You don’t read my mother’s novels, I suppose.… It was she who did all my father’s writing for him. He couldn’t write, he was too impatient!”

===Chinese===
「我哥哥和我准是古怪的一路人，」瓦伦丁说。「有这样一个父亲……当然，还有母亲！」

万诺斯特拉奇小姐说：

「哦……你的*母亲*……」

瓦伦丁当即在想象里召来了万诺斯特拉奇小姐年轻时那个小小的、崇拜成癖的女人圈子——她们全都在牛津星期天的树影下窥伺着她父亲和母亲的散步：父亲那样轻快神气、那样醒豁，母亲那样拖拖沓沓、宽宽大大、慷慨而不留意四周。那一小圈子人个个都在念叨：要是他身边照料他的是*我们*就好了。……她带几分恶意地说：

「我猜您是不读我母亲的小说的。……我父亲所有的文字，从头到尾都是她替他写成的。他自己不会写，他太没有耐性！」

===Original===
Miss Wanostrocht exclaimed:

“Oh, you *shouldn’t* say that!” with almost the pain of someone defending her own personal reputation.

“I don’t see why I shouldn’t,” Valentine said. “He was the first person to say it about himself.”

“He shouldn’t have said it either,” Miss Wanostrocht answered with a sort of soft unction. “He should have taken care more of his own reputation for the sake of his Work!”

Valentine considered this thin, ecstatic spinster with ironic curiosity.

===Chinese===
万诺斯特拉奇小姐惊呼起来：

「哦，你*不该*说这样的话！」那痛楚，几乎像有人在捍卫自己的名声。

「我不明白为什么不该，」瓦伦丁说。「头一个这么说他的，就是他自己。」

「他自己也不该说，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐带着一种软腻腻的虔诚口吻答道。「为了他的事业起见，他也该多爱惜一点自己的名声才是！」

瓦伦丁带着讥诮的好奇，打量着这位瘦削的、心醉神迷的老小姐。

===Original===
“Of course, if you’ve sat… if you’re still sitting at father’s feet as much as all that,” she conceded, “it gives you a certain right to be careful about his reputation.… All the same I wish you would tell me what that person said on the phone!”

The bust of Miss Wanostrocht moved with a sudden eagerness further towards the edge of her table.

“It’s precisely because of that,” she said, “that I want to speak to you first.… That I want you to consider.…”

===Chinese===
「当然，既然您曾坐在……既然您至今还这般坐在家父的膝下，」她让步道，「那您自然有几分权利，去操心他的名声。……尽管如此，我还是希望您能告诉我，那位人物在电话里都说了些什么！」

万诺斯特拉奇小姐的上半身带着一阵突然的急切，朝桌沿又凑近了些。

「恰恰是因为这个，」她说，「我才想先同你谈一谈。……才想请你考虑一下……」

===Original===
Valentine said:

“Because of my father’s reputation.… Look here, did that person—Lady Macmaster!—speak to you as if you were me? Our names are near enough to make it possible.”

“You’re,” Miss Wanostrocht said, “as one might say, the fine fruit of the product of his views on the education of women. And if you… It’s been such a satisfaction to me to observe in you such a… a sound, instructed head on such a… oh, you know, sane body.… And then.… An earning capacity. A commercial value. Your father, of course, never minced words.…” She added:

“I’m bound to say that my interview with Lady Macmaster… Who surely isn’t a lady of whom you could say that you disapprove. I’ve read her husband’s work. It surely—you’d say, wouldn’t you?—conserves some of the ancient fire.”

===Chinese===
瓦伦丁说：

「为了我父亲的名声。……我说，那位人物——麦克马斯特夫人！——跟您说话的时候，是不是把您当成了我？我们两人的名字相近得很，大有这个可能。」

「你呢，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐说，「可以说，是他那套女子教育见解的产物中结出的精华之果。而要是你……能在你身上观察到这样一颗……一颗健全的、受过教诲的头脑，安在那样一副……哦你知道的，健全的身体上，于我一直是一桩莫大的欣慰。……再者……一种谋生的能力。一种商业价值。令尊当然从来是有话直说的。……」她补充道：

「我不得不说，我与麦克马斯特夫人的那番谈话……她总归不是一位你能说自己看不惯的女人吧。我读过她丈夫的著作。那些著作——你会这么说，不是吗？——想必还保存着几分古代的火焰。」

===Original===
“He,” Valentine said, “hasn’t a word of Latin to his tail. He makes his quotations out, if he uses them, by means of school-cribs.… I know his methods of work, you know.”

It occurred to Valentine to think that if Edith Ethel really *had* at first taken Miss Wanostrocht for herself there might pretty obviously be some cause for Miss Wanostrocht’s concern for her father’s reputation as an intimate trainer of young women. She figured Edith Ethel suddenly bursting into a description of the circumstances of that man who was without furniture and did not appear to recognise the porter. The relations she might have described as having existed between her and him might well worry the Head of a Great Public School for Middle Class Girls. She had no doubt been described as having had a baby. A disagreeable and outraged current invaded her feelings.…

===Chinese===
「他呀，」瓦伦丁说，「连尾巴梢上都挂不住一个拉丁字。他若是引用，也是靠学堂用的拉丁文对照小抄拼凑出来的。……他的那一套工作方法，我是知道的。」

瓦伦丁忽然想到：倘若伊迪丝·埃塞尔起初当真*错把*万诺斯特拉奇小姐当成了她自己，那么这位小姐如此挂心她父亲作为年轻女性贴身导师的名声，缘由就相当明显了。她想象着伊迪丝·埃塞尔突然絮絮地描述起那个男人的境况——他没有家具，又似乎认不得公寓的门房——她与他之间可能被描述成存在过的那种关系，完全足以让一所中产阶级女子公学的校长忧心忡忡。十有八九，她已被描述成生过一个孩子了。一股不快的、愤懑的潜流侵入了她的心绪。……

===Original===
It was suddenly obscured by a recrudescence of the thought that had come to her only incidentally in the hall. It rushed over her with extraordinary vividness now, like a wave of warm liquid.… If it *had* really been that fellow’s wife who had removed his furniture what *was* there to keep them apart? He couldn’t have pawned or sold or burnt his furniture whilst he had been with the British Expeditionary Force in the Low Countries! He couldn’t have without extraordinary difficulty! Then… What *should* keep them apart?… Middle Class Morality? A pretty gory carnival that had been for the last four years! Was this then Lent, pressing hard on the heels of Saturnalia? Not so hard as that, surely! So that if one hurried.… What on earth did she want, unknown to herself?

===Chinese===
这心绪倏地又被另一个念头的复起淹没了——那念头先前在礼堂里只是偶然掠过她的心头。此刻它带着异乎寻常的鲜活向她涌来，像一股温热的液体。……倘若搬走那家伙家具的*当真*是他妻子，那还有什么*能*把他们隔开？他总不至于在随英国远征军驻扎低地国家期间，把自己的家具当了、卖了，或是烧了吧！没有超乎寻常的周折，他做不到这些！那么……究竟还有什么*该*把他们隔开？……中产阶级的道德？过去四年里，那玩意儿可一直是场血淋淋的狂欢！那么此刻莫非是大斋期（Lent），紧跟在农神节（Saturnalia）狂欢的脚后跟扑上来？总不至于紧迫到这个地步吧，肯定的！所以，要是抓紧点儿……她到底想要什么呢——连她自己也不知道？

===Original===
She heard herself saying, almost with a sob, so that she was evidently in a state of emotion:

“Look here: I disapprove of this whole thing: of what my father has brought me to! Those people… the brilliant Victorians talked all the time through their hats. They evolved a theory from anywhere and then went brilliantly mad over it. Perfectly recklessly.… Have you noticed Pettigul One?… Hasn’t it occurred to you that you *can’t* carry on violent physical jerks and mental work side by side? I ought not to be in this school and I ought not to be what I am!”

At Miss Wanostrocht’s perturbed expression she said to herself:

“What on earth am I saying all this for? You’d think I was trying to cut loose from this school! Am I?”

Nevertheless her voice was going on:

“There’s too much oxygenation of the lungs, here. It’s unnatural. It affects the brain, deleteriously. Pettigul One is an example of it. She’s earnest with me and earnest with her books. Now she’s gone dotty. Most of them it only stupefies.”

===Chinese===
她听见自己开了口，几乎带着哭腔，可见她分明已处在激动之中：

「听着：我对这整个事情都不以为然：对我父亲把我塑造成的这副田地，都不以为然！那些人……那些才华焕发的维多利亚时代人，成天信口开河。他们从随便什么地方都能演化出一套理论来，然后围绕着它痛痛快快地发起疯来。肆无忌惮到了极点。……您注意到佩蒂古尔老大了吗？……您就没有想到过，剧烈的体育操练和脑力劳动，是*不能*齐头并进的吗？我本不该待在这所学校里，也本不该是如今这个身份！」

见万诺斯特拉奇小姐面露不安，她暗自思忖：

「我到底说这些做什么？听上去倒像我要撇清这所学校似的！我是吗？」

然而她的声音还在往下说：

「这地方，肺部充氧太多了。这不自然。它损害大脑，为害不浅。佩蒂古尔老大就是个例子。她在我这儿用功，在她的书本上也用功。如今她疯了。对其余大多数人，这充其量只会把人变傻。」

===Original===
It was incredible to her that the mere imagination that that fellow’s wife had left him should make her spout out like this—for all the world like her father spouting out one of his ingenious theories!… It had really occurred to her once or twice to think that you could not run a dual physical and mental existence without some risk. The military physical developments of the last four years had been responsible for a real exaggeration of physical values. She was aware that in that Institution, for the last four years, she had been regarded as supplementing if not as actually replacing both the doctor and the priest.… But from that to evolving a complete theory that the Pettigul’s lie was the product of an over-oxygenated brain was going pretty far.…

Still, she was prevented from taking part in national rejoicings; pretty certainly Edith Ethel had been talking scandal about her to Miss Wanostrocht. She had the right to take it out in some sort of exaggerated declamation!

“It appears,” Miss Wanostrocht said, “for we can’t now go into the question of the whole curriculum of the school, though I am inclined to agree with you. What by the by is the matter with Pettigul One? I thought her rather a solid sort of girl. But it appears that the wife of a friend… perhaps it’s only a former friend of yours, is in a nursing home.”

Valentine exclaimed:

“Oh, he.… But that’s too ghastly!”

“It appears,” Miss Wanostrocht said, “to be rather a mess.” She added: “That appears to be the only expression to use.”

===Chinese===
叫她难以置信的是，单凭想象那人的妻子离开了他这么一件事，竟会让她这样滔滔不绝地发起议论来——活脱脱像她父亲滔滔不绝地发表他某一套别出心裁的理论！……她也的确有过一两回真的想到：要把肉体和精神的双重生活一并经营下去，不可能不担些风险。过去四年军事上的体能发展，确确实实造成了对身体价值的一种夸大。她明白，在那所学堂里，过去四年间，她一直被视为医生和神父二职的补充——倘若不是替代的话。……可是由此便演化出一套完整理论，说佩蒂古尔的谎话是大脑充氧过度的产物，这就走得太远了。……

话虽如此，她终究是被剥夺了参加举国欢庆的资格；十有八九是伊迪丝·埃塞尔在万诺斯特拉奇小姐面前编派了她的丑闻。她有权借一番夸张的慷慨陈词，把这口气出出来！

「看来，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐说，「——学校整个课程的问题，我们眼下无从深究，虽然我倒倾向于同意你的看法。顺便问一句，佩蒂古尔老大到底是怎么回事？我原以为她是个挺扎实的姑娘。可是看来，你一位朋友的妻子……也许那只能算是个旧交了，眼下正住在一家疗养院里。」

瓦伦丁失声道：

「哦，他。……那太可怕了！」

「看来，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐说，「事情相当糟糕。」她补了一句：「看来，这是唯一可用的措辞。」

---

===Original===
For Valentine, that piece of news threw a blinding light upon herself. She was overwhelmingly appalled because that woman was in a nursing home. Because in that case it would not be sporting to go and see the husband!

Miss Wanostrocht went on:

“Lady Macmaster was anxious for your advice.… It appears that the only other person that could look after the interests of… of your friend: his brother…”

Valentine missed something out of that sentence. Miss Wanostrocht talked too fluently. If people wanted you to appreciate items of sledgehammering news they should not use long sentences. They should say:

“He’s mad and penniless. His brother’s dying: his wife’s just been operated on.” Like that! Then you could take it in; even if your mind was rioting about like a cat in a barrel.

===Chinese===
对瓦伦丁来说，这条消息像一道炫目的光，把她自己照了个通亮。她惊骇万分，只因为那个女人进了疗养院。因为若是那样，再去看望那位丈夫，就算不得光明正大了！

万诺斯特拉奇小姐继续说：

「麦克马斯特夫人急欲征求你的意见。……看来，另外唯一能够照看……照看你那位朋友权益的人：他的兄弟……」

这句话里有个环节瓦伦丁没有接住。万诺斯特拉奇小姐说得太溜了。人们若要你领会一记记重锤似的消息，就不该用长句子。就该说：

「他疯了，身无分文。他兄弟快死了：他妻子刚动了手术。」这样才对！这样你才吃得消——哪怕你的脑子正像桶里的猫一样闹腾个不停。

===Original===
“The brother’s… female companion,” Miss Wanostrocht was wandering on, “though it appears that she would have been willing is therefore not available.… The theory is that he—he himself, your friend, has been considerably unhinged by his experiences in the war. Then.… Who in your opinion should take the responsibility of looking after his interests?”

Valentine heard herself say:

“Me!”

She added:

“Him! Looking after him. I don’t know that he has any… interests!”

He didn’t appear to have any furniture, so how could he have the other things. She wished Miss Wanostrocht would leave off using the word “appear.” It was irritating… and infectious. Could the lady not make a direct statement? But then, no one ever made clear statements and this no doubt appeared to that anæmic spinster a singularly tenebrous affair.

===Chinese===
「那位兄弟的……女伴，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐兀自絮絮地说下去，「虽然看来她本也愿意，如今却因此腾不出身了。……据猜想，他——你那位朋友本人——已被战争中的经历大大搞坏了心神。那么。……依你看，该由谁来负起照看他权益的责任呢？」

瓦伦丁听见自己说：

「我！」

她又说：

「他！由我来照看他。我不知道他还有什么……权益可言！」

他看来连家具都没有，别的东西还能有什么呢。她只盼万诺斯特拉奇小姐别再用「看来」这个词了。听着叫人恼火……还会传染。这位女士难道就不能把话说得直截了当？可是话说回来，从来没有人把话说清楚过，而这桩事，在那位贫血质的老小姐看来，无疑显得格外幽晦。

===Original===
As for clear statements.… If there had ever been any in precisely this tenebrous mess she, Valentine, would know how she stood with that man’s wife. For it was part of the preposterous way in which she herself and all her friends behaved that they never made clear statements—except for Edith Ethel who had the nature of a female costermonger and could not tell the truth, though she could be clear enough. But even Edith Ethel had never hitherto said anything about the way the wife in this case treated the husband. She had given Valentine very clearly to understand that she “sided” with the wife—but she had never gone as far as to say that the wife was a good wife. If she—Valentine—could only know that.

Miss Wanostrocht was asking:

“When you say ‘Me,’ do you mean that you would propose to look after that man yourself? I trust not.”

===Chinese===
至于把话说清楚。……倘若在这团幽晦的乱麻里当真有过什么清楚明白的话，她瓦伦丁早就会知道，在那个男人的妻子面前，自己究竟站在什么位置了。因为，从来不说清楚的话，本就是她自己和她所有朋友行事荒唐的一部分——唯独伊迪丝·埃塞尔例外：她天生一副女小贩的性子，说不出真话，倒能把话说得够清楚。可就连伊迪丝·埃塞尔，迄今也从未说过本案中那位妻子是怎样对待那位丈夫的。她让瓦伦丁清清楚楚地明白，她是「站在」妻子一边的——但她从不曾更进一步，说那位妻子是个好妻子。她——瓦伦丁——要是能知道这个就好了。

万诺斯特拉奇小姐正在问：

「你说『我』，是不是意味着你打算亲自去照看那个人？但愿不是。」

===Original===
… Because, obviously, if she were a good wife, she, Valentine couldn’t butt in… not generously. As her father’s and still more her mother’s daughter.… On the face of it you would say that a wife who was always striding along the palings of the Row, or the paths of other resorts of the fashionable could not be a good—a domestic—wife for a Statistician. On the other hand he was a pretty smart man, Governing class, county family and the rest of it—so he might like his wife to figure in Society: he might even exact it. He was quite capable of that. Why, for all she knew, the wife might be a retiring, shy person whom he thrust out into the hard world. It was not likely: but it was as possible as anything else.

Miss Wanostrocht was asking:

“Aren’t there Institutions… Military Sanatoria… for cases precisely like that of this Captain Tietjens. It appears to be the war that has broken him down, not merely evil living.”

“It’s precisely,” Valentine said, “because of that that one should want… shouldn’t one.… Because it’s because of the War…”

The sentence would not finish itself.

Miss Wanostrocht said:

“I thought.… It has been represented to me… that you were a Pacifist. Of an extreme type!”

===Chinese===
……因为，很显然，倘若她是个好妻子，她瓦伦丁就不能插足进去……不能厚道地插足。身为她父亲的、更其是她母亲的女儿。……照表面看，你会说，一个总是沿着骑马道（the Row）的围栏大步而行、或出没于时髦人物其他聚集地的妻子，不可能是一位统计学家（Statistician）的好的——安于家室的——妻子。可另一方面，他也是个相当精明的人物，统治阶层，郡绅世家，诸如此类——所以他兴许乐意妻子在社交界抛头露面：他甚至可能苛求这一点。他完全干得出来。可不是嘛，天知道，那位妻子没准是个畏缩腼腆的人，是他把她硬推进了这个冷酷世界。这不太可能：可它跟别的任何可能性一样可能。

万诺斯特拉奇小姐正在问：

「难道就没有一些机构……军队疗养院……专门收容恰如这位蒂金斯上尉这般的病例。看来把他压垮的是这场战争，而不单是品行不端。」

「恰恰是因为这个，」瓦伦丁说，「人才更应该……难道不应该么。……因为正是因为这场战争……」

这个句子自己不肯收尾。

万诺斯特拉奇小姐说：

「我还以为。……有人向我陈说……你是个和平主义者。还是极端的那一型！」

===Original===
It had given Valentine a turn—like the breaking out of sweat in a case of fever—to hear the name, coldly: “Captain Tietjens,” for it was like a release. She had been irrationally determined that hers should not be the first tongue to utter that name.

And apparently from her tone Miss Wanostrocht was prepared to detest that Captain Tietjens. Perhaps she detested him already.

She was beginning to say:

“If one is an extreme Pacifist because one cannot bear to think of the sufferings of men isn’t that a precise reason why one should wish that a poor devil, all broken up…”

But Miss Wanostrocht had begun one of her own long sentences. Their voices went on together, like trains dragging along ballast—disagreeably. Miss Wanostrocht’s organ, however, won out with the words:

“… behaved very badly indeed.”

Valentine said hotly:

“You ought not to believe anything of the sort—on the strength of anything said by a woman like Lady Macmaster.”

===Chinese===
听到那名字被冷冷地吐出来——「蒂金斯上尉」——瓦伦丁猛地一激灵，就像热病发作时忽然出了一身汗，因为这仿佛是一种解脱。她一直存着一股不讲道理的执拗：她的舌头绝不该是头一个吐出这个名字的。

而且从口气听来，万诺斯特拉奇小姐分明是打定了主意要憎恶那位蒂金斯上尉。说不定她已经憎恶上了。

她正开始说：

「倘若一个人因为不忍去想众人所受的苦难而成了极端的和平主义者，这难道不正是她该希望一个被彻底压垮的可怜虫……」

可万诺斯特拉奇小姐已开始了她自己的一句长句子。两人的声音一齐往前赶，像两列火车沿道砟拖行——煞是不快。不过万诺斯特拉奇小姐的风琴嗓终究胜出，吐出这样几个字：

「……的确表现得很不堪。」

瓦伦丁愤然道：

「您不该相信任何这一类的话——凭麦克马斯特夫人那种女人所说的任何话。」

===Original===
Miss Wanostrocht appeared to have been brought to a complete stop: she leaned forward in her chair; her mouth was a little open. And Valentine said: “Thank Goodness!” to herself.

She had to have a moment to herself to digest what had the air of being new evidence of the baseness of Edith Ethel; she felt herself to be infuriated in regions of her own being that she hardly knew. That seemed to her to be a littleness in herself. She had not thought that she had been as little as that. It ought not to matter what people said of you. She was perfectly accustomed to think of Edith Ethel as telling whole crowds of people very bad things about her, Valentine Wannop. But there was about this a recklessness that was hardly believable. To tell an unknown person, encountered by chance on the telephone, derogatory facts about a third party who might be expected to come to the telephone herself in a minute or two—and, not only that—who must in all probability hear what had been said very soon after, from the first listener.… That was surely a recklessness of evil-speaking that almost outpassed sanity.… Or else it betrayed a contempt for her, Valentine Wannop, and what she could do in the way of reprisals that was extremely hard to bear!

===Chinese===
万诺斯特拉奇小姐像是被彻底堵住了：她在椅子上俯身向前，嘴巴微微张着。瓦伦丁心里说了声「谢天谢地！」

她需要独自缓上一刻，好消化这桩看上去是伊迪丝·埃塞尔卑劣行径的新证据的事；她觉得自己心头有几分连她自己都几乎不识得的角落正在冒火。这在她看来，是自己身上的一种小气。她不曾想到自己竟小气到这个地步。人家说你什么，本不该放在心上。她早已惯于把伊迪丝·埃塞尔想象成在成群成群的人们面前，讲她瓦伦丁·万诺普的坏话。可这件事里有一种几乎令人难以置信的鲁莽轻率。对一个在电话里偶然碰上的陌生人，讲述一个第三者的种种丑事——而这第三本人说不定一两分钟后就会自己来接电话——还不止如此——她还十有八九很快就会从第一个听者嘴里听到这些话。……这造起谣来可真是鲁莽得几乎越出了神志的边界。……再不然，这就暴露出一种对她瓦伦丁·万诺普的轻蔑——轻蔑她在报复手段上能有什么作为——这实在叫人难以忍受！

===Original===
She said suddenly to Miss Wanostrocht:

“Look here! Are you speaking to me as a friend to my father’s daughter or as a Headmistress to a Physical Instructor?”

A certain amount of blood came into the lady’s pinkish features. She had certainly been ruffled when Valentine had permitted her voice to sound so long alongside her own; for, although Valentine knew next to nothing about the Head’s likes or dislikes she had once or twice before seen her evince marked distaste on being interrupted in one of her formal sentences.

Miss Wanostrocht said with a certain coldness:

“I’m speaking at present.… I’m allowing myself the liberty—as a much older woman—in the capacity of a friend of your father. I have been, in short, trying to recall to you all that you owe to yourself as being an example of his training!”

===Chinese===
她突然对万诺斯特拉奇小姐说：

「我说！您这是拿我当我父亲朋友的女儿说话呢，还是拿我当校长手下的体育教员说话？」

这位女士微红的脸上又泛起了一层血色。方才瓦伦丁让自己的嗓音跟她的嗓音齐鸣了那么久，她确实被惹恼了；因为虽说瓦伦丁对校长的好恶几乎一无所知，她却见过一两次：她在一句字斟句酌的句子中间被打断时，露出显著的不悦。

万诺斯特拉奇小姐带着几分冷淡说：

「此刻我是在说话。……我是在容许自己冒昧——以一个年长许多的女性的身份——以令尊故友的身份。简而言之，我一直想要提醒你的，是：身为他那套教育的一个典范，你对自己负有多少亏欠！」

===Original===
Involuntarily Valentine’s lips formed themselves for a low whistle of incredulity. She said to herself:

“By Jove! I am in the middle of a nasty affair.… This is a sort of professional cross-examination.”

“I am in a way glad,” the lady was now continuing, “that you take that line.… I mean of defending Mrs. Tietjens with such heat against Lady Macmaster. Lady Macmaster appears to dislike Mrs. Tietjens, but I am bound to say that she appears to be in the right of it. I mean of her dislike. Lady Macmaster is a serious personality and, even on her public record Mrs. Tietjens appears to be very much the reverse. No doubt you wish to be loyal to your… friends, but…”

===Chinese===
瓦伦丁的嘴唇不由自主地撮了起来，要吹一声表示不信的低低口哨。她心想：

「天哪！我正卷在一桩麻烦事当中。……这简直是一场职业性的盘问。」

「在某种意义上我倒高兴，」这位女士接着往下说，「你采取了那个立场。……我是说，你那样激烈地替蒂金斯太太辩护，顶撞麦克马斯特夫人。麦克马斯特夫人似乎不喜欢蒂金斯太太，可我不得不说，她似乎不无道理。我是说，她的不喜欢不无道理。麦克马斯特夫人是个庄重的人物，而蒂金斯太太，单凭她公开的履历来看，似乎便恰得其反。无疑你想忠于你的……朋友们，可是……」

===Original===
“We appear,” Valentine said, “to be getting into an extraordinary muddle.”

She added:

“I haven’t, as you seem to think, been defending Mrs. Tietjens. I would have. I would at any time. I have always thought of her as beautiful and kind. But I heard you say the words: ‘*has been behaving very badly*,’ and I thought you meant that Captain Tietjens had. I denied it. If you meant that his wife has, I deny it, too. She’s an admirable wife… and mother… that sort of thing, for all I know.…”

She said to herself:

“Now why do I say that? What’s Hecuba to me?” and then:

“It’s to defend *his* honour, of course… I’m trying to present Captain Tietjens as English Country Gentleman complete with admirably arranged establishment, stables, kennels, spouse, offspring… That’s a queer thing to want to do!”

===Chinese===
「我们似乎，」瓦伦丁说，「正陷进一场非同小可的混乱里。」

她又说：

「我并不像您以为的那样，一直在替蒂金斯太太辩护。我本来会的。我随时都会。我一向认为她美丽而仁慈。可我听见您说出这几个字：『*一直表现得很不堪*』，我以为您指的是蒂金斯上尉表现得很不堪。我否认了。倘若您指的是他妻子，我也同样否认。她是位无可挑剔的妻子……和母亲……诸如此类，据我所知……」

她心里自问：

「现在我干吗要说这些？赫卡柏（Hecuba）与我何干？」随即又想：

「这自然是为了捍卫*他*的名誉……我这是在努力把蒂金斯上尉呈现成一名英国乡村绅士：家宅安排得无可挑剔，马厩、狗舍、配偶、子嗣一应俱全……想这么做可真是一桩怪事！」

===Original===
Miss Wanostrocht who had breathed deeply said now:

“I’m extremely glad to hear that. Lady Macmaster certainly said that Mrs. Tietjens was—let us say—at least a neglectful wife.… Vain, you know; idle; overdressed.… All that… And you appeared to defend Mrs. Tietjens.”

“She’s a smart woman in smart Society,” Valentine said, “but it’s with her husband’s concurrence. She has a right to be.…”

“We shouldn’t,” Miss Wanostrocht said, “be in the extraordinary muddle to which you referred if you did not so continually interrupt me. I was trying to say that, for you, an inexperienced girl, brought up in a sheltered home, no pitfall could be more dangerous than a man with a wife who neglected her duties!”

Valentine said:

“You will have to excuse my interrupting you. It *is*, you know, rather more my funeral than yours.”

Miss Wanostrocht said quickly:

“You can’t say that. You don’t know how ardently.…”

Valentine said:

“Yes, yes.… Your *schwaerm* for my father’s memory and all.… But my father couldn’t bring it about that I should lead a sheltered life.… I’m about as experienced as any girl of the lower classes.… No doubt it was his doing, but don’t make any mistakes.”

She added:

“Still, it’s I that’s the corpse. You’re conducting the inquest. So it’s more fun for you.”

===Chinese===
万诺斯特拉奇小姐深吸过一口气，这时说：

「听到这话我极为欣慰。麦克马斯特夫人确实说过，蒂金斯太太——我们姑且这么说——至少是个疏于职守的妻子。……虚荣，你知道；懒散；衣着一味铺张。……诸如此类……而你当时像是在替蒂金斯太太辩护。」

「她是时髦社交界里一个时髦的女人，」瓦伦丁说，「可她出入其间是有丈夫首肯的。她有这个权利……」

「倘若你不是这样不停地打断我，」万诺斯特拉奇小姐说，「我们本不至于陷进你所说的这场非同小可的混乱。我方才想说的是：对你这样一个毫无阅历的、在荫庇之家养大的女孩子，再没有哪个陷阱，比一个有着失职妻子的男人更危险的了！」

瓦伦丁说：

「您得原谅我打断您。您知道的，这*毕竟*更像是我的葬礼，而不是您的。」

万诺斯特拉奇小姐急忙说：

「你不能这么说。你不知道我多么热切地……」

瓦伦丁说：

「是，是。……您对我父亲亡灵的那份*倾慕*（schwaerm），还有种种。……可我父亲没能让我过上深闺生活。……我的阅历，跟下层阶级随便哪个姑娘不相上下。……这无疑是他的手笔，可您千万别弄错了。」

她又说：

「不过，躺着的是我，您办的是验尸。所以说，有意思的是您。」

===Original===
Miss Wanostrocht had grown slightly pale:

“I; if.…” she stammered slightly, “by ‘experience’ you mean.…”

“I don’t,” Valentine exclaimed, “and you have no right to infer that I do on the strength of a conversation you’ve had, but shouldn’t have had, with one of the worst tongues in London.… I mean that my father left us so that I had to earn my and my mother’s living as a servant for some months after his death. That was what his training came to. But I can look after myself… In consequence.…”

Miss Wanostrocht had thrown herself back in her chair.

“But…” she exclaimed: she had grown completely pale—like discoloured wax. “There was a subscription.… We.…” she began again: “We knew that he hadn’t.…”

“You subscribed,” Valentine said, “to purchase his library and presented it to his wife… who had nothing to eat but what my wages as a tweeny maid got for her.” But before the pallor of the other lady she tried to add a touch of generosity: “Of course the subscribers wanted, very naturally, to preserve as much as they could of his personality. A man’s books are very much himself. That was all right.” She added: “All the same I had that training: in a suburban basement. So you cannot teach me a great deal about the shady in life. I was in the family of a Middlesex County Councillor. In Ealing.”

===Chinese===
万诺斯特拉奇小姐的脸色微微发白了：

「我；要是……」她微微结巴起来，「你说『阅历』，指的是……」

「不是那个意思，」瓦伦丁失声道，「您也无权凭您跟伦敦数得着的一条坏舌头进行过的那场——本不该有的——谈话，就推断我是那个意思。……我的意思是：我父亲撇下我们走了，害得我在他死后有几个月只能出去当女仆，靠打工挣下我和我母亲的口粮。他的那套教育，到头来落了个这。不过我能照管我自己……正因为如此……」

万诺斯特拉奇小姐整个人向后靠进了椅子里。

「可是……」她喊道：她已经完全变了色——像褪了色的蜡。「当年是有一场认捐的……我们……」她重新开口：「我们当时就知道他没有……」

「你们认了捐，」瓦伦丁说，「凑钱买下他的藏书，赠给了他的遗孀……而她除了我当打杂女仆（tweeny maid）挣来的工钱，再没有一口吃食。」不过眼看对方女士那副惨白，她竭力添上一分厚道：「当然，认捐的人非常自然地想尽量多保存一点他这个人的痕迹。一个人的书，在很大程度上就是他本人。那没有什么不对。」她补一句：「话虽如此，我可是受过那种历练的：在郊区一栋房子的地下层里。所以关于生活中见不得人的那些事，您教不了我多少。我当过差的那家，是米德尔塞克斯郡一位郡议员的家。在伊灵（Ealing）。」

===Original===
Miss Wanostrocht said faintly:

“This is very dreadful!”

“It isn’t really!” Valentine said. “I wasn’t badly treated as tweeny maids go. It would have been better if the Mistress hadn’t been a constant invalid and the cook constantly drunk.… After that I did a little office work. For the suffragettes. That was after old Mr. Tietjens came back from abroad and gave mother some work on a paper he owned. We scrambled along then, somehow. Old Mr. Tietjens was father’s greatest friend, so father’s side, as you might say, turned up trumps—If you like to think that to console you.…”

Miss Wanostrocht was bending her face down over her table, presumably to hide a little of it from Valentine or to avoid the girl’s eyes.

===Chinese===
万诺斯特拉奇小姐有气无力地说：

「这太可怕了！」

「其实并没有！」瓦伦丁说。「就打杂女仆而论，我受的待遇不算坏。若不是女主人终年缠绵病榻、厨娘终日烂醉如泥，本会更好些。……那之后我做过一点文书差事。替妇女参政论者们（suffragettes）干的。那是在蒂金斯老先生从国外回来、在他名下的一家报馆里给了母亲一点活计之后。那时候我们总算熬出个头绪来了。蒂金斯老先生是家父最好的朋友，所以家父这一边，可以说，到头来亮出了王牌——您若愿意这么想，好宽一宽心……」

万诺斯特拉奇小姐把脸伏在她的桌子上，多半是想从瓦伦丁跟前遮去几分脸色，或是躲开这姑娘的目光。

===Original===
Valentine went on:

“One knows all about the conflict between a man’s private duties and his public achievements. But with a very little less of the flamboyant in his life my father might have left us very much better off. It isn’t what I *want*—to be a cross between a sergeant in the army and an upper housemaid. Any more than I wanted to be an under one.”

Miss Wanostrocht uttered an “Oh!” of pain. She exclaimed rapidly:

“It was your moral rather than your mere athletic influence that made me so glad to have you here.… It was because I felt that you did not set such a high value on the physical.…”

===Chinese===
瓦伦丁继续说：

「一个人的私人义务同他的公共成就之间的冲突，人人都知道是怎么回事。可要是我父亲生平的浮艳之气哪怕稍稍收敛几分，他本可以给我们留下宽裕得多的境况。一半像军队里的中士、一半像上等女管家的这么个两不像，可不是我*想要*的——正如我原先也不想要当下等的女仆。」

万诺斯特拉奇小姐发出一声痛苦的「哦！」。她急速地说：

「让我如此庆幸有你在这儿的，是你的道德感化力，而不单是你体育上的影响力。……那是因为我感到，你并不怎么高看肉体……」

===Original===
“Well, you aren’t going to have me here much longer,” Valentine said. “Not an instant more than I can in decency help. I’m going to.…”

She said to herself:

“What on earth am I going to do?… What do I want?”

She wanted to lie in a hammock beside a blue, tideless sea and think about Tibullus… There was no nonsense about her. She did not want to engage in intellectual pursuits herself. She had not the training. But she intended to enjoy the more luxurious forms of the intellectual products of others.… That appeared to be the moral of the day!

===Chinese===
「话说回来，您留不住我多久了，」瓦伦丁说。「只要体面上勉强过得去，我一刻也不多待。我打算……」

她心里自问：

「我到底要做什么？……我想要什么？」

她想躺在一张吊床里，傍着一片蔚蓝的、无潮的海，想想提布卢斯（Tibullus）……她这人没有半点虚妄。她自己并不想从事智识的营生。她没受过那个训练。但她打算享受旁人智识产品中较为奢华的那几种。……看来，这便是这一天的教益！

===Original===
And, looking rather minutely at Miss Wanostrocht’s inclined face, she wondered if, in the history of the world, there had ever been such another day. Had Miss Wanostrocht, for instance, ever known what it was to have a man come back. Ah, but amid the tumult of a million other men coming back! A collective impulse to slacken off! Immense! Softening!

Miss Wanostrocht had apparently loved her father. No doubt in company with fifty damsels. Did they ever get a collective kick out of that affair? It was even possible that she had spoken as she had… *pour cause*. Warning her, Valentine, against the deleterious effect of being connected with a man whose wife was unsatisfactory.… Because the fifty damsels had all, in duty bound, thought that her mother was an unsatisfactory wife for the brilliant, grey-black haired Eminence with the figure of a stripling that her father had been.… They had probably thought that, without the untidy figure of Mrs. Wannop as a weight upon him, he might have become.… Well, with one of *them*!… Anything! Any sort of figure in the councils of the nation. Why not Prime Minister? For along with his pedagogic theories he had had political occupations. He had certainly had the friendship of Disraeli. He supplied—it was historic!—materials for eternally famous, meretricious speeches. He would have been head-trainer of the Empire’s proconsuls if the other fellow, at Balliol, had not got in first.… As it was he had had to specialise in the Education of Women. Building up Primrose Dames.…

So Miss Wanostrocht warned her against the deleterious effect of neglected wives upon young, attached virgins! It probably *was* deleterious. Where would she, Valentine Wannop have been by now if she had thought that Sylvia Tietjens was really a bad one?

===Chinese===
而且，细细端详着万诺斯特拉奇小姐那张俯倾的脸，她不禁疑惑：世界史上，可曾有过另外一个这样的日子？譬如说，万诺斯特拉奇小姐可曾尝过有个男人回家的滋味。啊，可这是在另外一百万个男人回家的喧嚣之中！一种集体松弛下来的冲动！浩大！令人酥软！

万诺斯特拉奇小姐显然爱过她父亲。无疑是在五十位少女的陪伴之下。她们可曾从那段情事里得到过什么集体的快感？甚至有可能，她这番话是事出有因（*pour cause*）才那么说的——警告她瓦伦丁，提防跟一个妻子不称职的男人扯上关系的种种坏处。……因为那五十位少女，个个都出于职分，认定她母亲配不上她父亲——那位头发黑中带灰、身量宛如少年的显赫才子。……她们八成在想：若没有万诺普太太这团不修边幅的身影坠着他，他本可以成为……唔，成为*她们当中某一个*的什么！……什么都成！国家议事堂里的任何一种人物。为什么当不了首相？因为除了他那套教育理论，他还从事过政治。他确实与迪斯累利（Disraeli）有过交情。他供给过——这是有史可稽的！——那些千古留名而华而不实的演说所需的材料。倘若不是贝利奥尔学院（Balliol）的那另一位抢先一步，他本会成为帝国诸位总督的总教习。……事到如今，他只落得专攻女子教育。培育出一位位樱草贵妇（Primrose Dames，保守党樱草联盟之女会员）……

就这样，万诺斯特拉奇小姐警告她，提防失职妻子们对动了情的年轻处女的坏影响！这影响多半*确实*有害。她瓦伦丁·万诺普若是当初当真认为西尔维娅·蒂金斯是个坏妻子，此刻又会沦落到什么田地？

===Original===
Miss Wanostrocht said, as if with sudden anxiety:

“You are going to do what? You propose to do what?”

Valentine said:

“Obviously after your conversation with Edith Ethel you won’t be so glad to have me here. My moral influence has not been brightened in aspect!” A wave of passionate resentment swept over her.

“Look here,” she said, “if you think that I am prepared to.…”

She stopped however. “No,” she said, “I am not going to introduce the housemaid note. But you will probably see that this is irritating.” She added: “I would have the case of Pettigul One looked into, if I were you. It might become epidemic in a big school like this. And we’ve no means of knowing where we stand nowadays!”

===Chinese===
万诺斯特拉奇小姐带着突如其来的焦虑说：

「你要去做什么？你打算去做什么？」

瓦伦丁说：

「同伊迪丝·埃塞尔那番谈话之后，您显然不会再那么乐意留我在这里了。我这份道德感化力，成色上可是黯淡了！」一股狂热的愤懑扫过她全身。

「我说，」她说，「您要是以为我打算……」

她却停住了。「不，」她说，「我可不打算扯起使女腔。可您多半也看得出，这事叫人恼火。」她补上一句：「我要是您，就会让人去查一查佩蒂古尔老大这件事。在这么大的一所学校里，它没准会闹成流行病。再说，如今的世道，咱们谁说得清自己站在哪儿！」

## Part II / 第二部

### I / 第一章

===Original===
Months and months before Christopher Tietjens had stood extremely wishing that his head were level with a particular splash of purposeless whitewash. Something behind his mind forced him to the conviction that, if his head—and of course the rest of his trunk and lower limbs—were suspended by a process of levitation to that distance above the duckboard on which, now, his feet were, he would be in an inviolable sphere. These waves of conviction recurred continually: he was constantly glancing aside and upwards at that splash: it was in the shape of the comb of a healthy rooster; it gleamed, with five serrations, in the just-beginning light that shone along the thin, unroofed channel in the gravel slope. Wet half-light, just filtering; more visible there than in the surrounding desolation because the deep, narrow channel framed a section of just-illuminated rift in the watery eastwards!

Twice he had stood up on a rifleman’s step enforced by a bully-beef case to look over—in the last few minutes. Each time, on stepping down again, he had been struck by that phenomenon: the light seen from the trench seemed if not brighter, then more definite. So, from the bottom of a pit-shaft in broad day you can see the stars. The wind was light, but from the North West. They had there the weariness of a beaten army: the weariness of having to begin always new days again.…

He glanced aside and upwards: that cockscomb of phosphorescence.… He felt waves of some X force propelling his temples towards it. He wondered if perhaps the night before he had not observed that that was a patch of reinforced concrete, therefore more resistant. He might of course have observed that and then forgotten it. He hadn’t! It was therefore irrational.

If you are lying down under fire—flat under pretty smart fire—and you have only a paper bag in front of your head for cover you feel immeasurably safer than you do without it. You have a mind at rest. This must be the same thing.

===Chinese===
好多个月以前，克里斯托弗·蒂金斯就已经站在那里，强烈地盼着自己的头能和一小片无端刷上去的白灰齐平。他脑子背后有什么东西逼着他相信：倘若他的头——当然还有躯干和下肢的其余部分——凭一种凌空悬浮之术，吊到那道高度，吊到他此刻双脚所踩的木排道（duckboard）上方那么高，他就能置身于一个不可侵犯的球体里。这股股信念不断重来：他不住地斜眼向上瞟那片白灰：它形似一只健壮公鸡的冠子，带着五个锯齿，在刚刚亮起的光里微微发亮——那道光沿着砾石坡上一条薄薄的、没有顶盖的沟槽照进来。湿漉漉的微明，刚能滤过；那儿比四周的荒凉里更看得清楚，因为这条又深又窄的沟槽，正好框住了水汽迷蒙的东方一道初亮的天缝！

几分钟里，他已经两次踩上射击踏台——底下垫了只咸牛肉罐头箱加高——探头向外张望。每回再跨下来，他都被那个现象打动：从堑壕里望出去的光，就算不更亮，也更确凿。就像大白天站在矿井井底，你能看见星星。风很轻，却是西北风。他们那儿有一种打了败仗的军队才有的疲惫：不得不总是重新开始新的一天的疲惫。……

他斜眼往上一瞟：那鸡冠似的磷光。……他觉得一阵阵什么 X 力量正推着他的太阳穴向它靠去。他寻思，说不定头天夜里他压根儿没留意到那是一片钢筋混凝土，因而格外经打。当然喽，他也可能留意到了、转眼又忘了。他没有！可见这念头毫无理性可言。

要是你趴在火力底下——被相当猛的火力压得平平地趴着——脑袋前头只挡着一只纸袋当掩护，你也会觉得比没有它安全了不知多少倍。心里就安生了。想必就是同一回事。

===Original===
It remained dark and quiet. It was forty-five minutes: it became forty-four… forty-three… forty-two minutes and thirty seconds before a crucial moment and the slate grey cases of miniature metal pineapples had not come from the bothering place.… Who knew if there was anyone in charge there?

Twice that night he had sent runners back. No results yet. That bothering fellow might quite well have forgotten to leave a substitute. That was not likely. A careful man. But a man with a mania might forget. Still it was not likely!…

Thoughts menaced him as clouds threaten the heads of mountains but for the moment they kept away. It was quiet; the wet cool air was agreeable. They had autumn mornings that felt like that in Yorkshire. The wheels of his physique moved smoothly; he was more free in the chest than he had been for months.

===Chinese===
天还黑着，还静着。还剩四十五分钟：变成四十四……四十三……四十二分三十秒，就到那个要命的时刻，而那几箱石板灰色的迷你金属菠萝还没有从那个叫人心焦的地方送来。……谁知道那儿究竟有没有人管事？

当夜他已经两次派传令兵往后送信。还没有回音。那个叫人心焦的家伙很可能忘了留个替手。这不太可能。是个细心人。可一个心里揣着执念的人是可能忘的。不过还是不太可能！……

种种念头像乌云压着山头似的威胁他，好在眼下它们还避在一边。四下安静；湿冷的空气很宜人。约克郡的秋天早晨就有这个味道。他这架身体的轮子转得顺滑；胸口比几个月来都畅快。

===Original===
A single immense cannon, at a tremendous distance said something. Something sulky. Aroused in its sleep and protesting. But it was not a signal to begin anything. Too heavy. Firing at something at a tremendous distance. At Paris, may be: or the North Pole: or the moon! They were capable of that, those fellows!

It would be a tremendous piece of frightfulness to hit the moon. Great gain in prestige. And useless. There was no knowing what they would not be up to, as long as it was stupid and useless. And, naturally boring.… And it was a mistake to be boring. One went on fighting to get rid of those bores—as you would to get rid of a bore in a club.

It was more descriptive to call what had spoken a cannon than a gun—though it was not done in the best local circles. It was all right to call seventy-fives or the implements of the horse artillery “guns”; they were mobile and toy-like. But those immense things were cannons; the sullen muzzles always elevated. Sullen, like cathedral dignitaries or butlers. The thickness of barrel compared to the bore appeared enormous as they pointed at the moon, or Paris, or Nova Scotia.

===Chinese===
一门其大无比的加农炮，在极远极远的地方说了句话。一句赌气的话。睡梦里被吵醒，正抗议呢。可它不是什么开场的信号。太重了。朝极远处的什么东西开火。兴许是朝巴黎：要么朝北极：要么朝月亮！那帮家伙干得出来！

要是打中了月亮，那可是天大的一桩威吓杰作。威望大赚。然则毫无用处。只要事情够蠢够无用，就保不齐他们干不出什么来。而且，自然而然，无聊。……而无聊是个错误。人要接着打这场仗，就是为了甩掉这帮闷货——就像你要甩掉俱乐部里的某个闷货一样。

把方才发言的那位称作「加农炮」比称作「火炮」更传神——尽管在上流的本地圈子里没人这么叫。七五炮啦、马拉炮兵的那些家伙什啦，叫「火炮」没毛病；它们轻捷，像玩具。可那些庞然大物是加农炮；阴沉的炮口永远仰着。阴沉，像座堂里的教士贵人，或像大宅的管家。炮管对炮膛的粗细之比，在它们指着月亮、指着巴黎或新斯科舍的时候，显得其大无比。

===Original===
Well, that cannon had not announced anything except itself! It was not the beginning of any barrage; our own fellows were not pooping off to shut it up. It had just announced itself, saying protestingly, “**Can… non**,” and its shell soaring away to an enormous height caught the reflection of the unrisen sun on its base. A shining disc, like a halo in flight.… Pretty! A pretty motive for a decoration, tiny pretty planes up on a blue sky amongst shiny, flying haloes! Dragon flies amongst saints.… No, “with angels and archangels!”… Well, one had seen it!

Cannon.… Yes, that was the right thing to call them. Like the upended, rusted things that stuck up out of parades when one had been a child.

No, not the signal for a barrage! A good thing! One might as well say “Thank Goodness,” for the later they began the less long it lasted.… Less long it lasted was ugly alliteration. Sooner it was over was better.… No doubt half-past eight or at half-past eight to the stroke those boring fellows would let off their usual offering, probably plump, right on top of that spot.… As far as one could tell three salvoes of a dozen shells each at half minute intervals between the salvoes. Perhaps salvoes was not the right word. Damn all artillery, anyhow!

Why did those fellows do it? Every morning at half-past eight; every afternoon at half-past two. Presumably just to show that they were still alive, and still boring. They were methodical. That was their secret. The secret of their boredom. Trying to kill them was like trying to shut up Liberals who would talk party politics in a nonpolitical club… had to be done, though! Otherwise the world was no place for… Oh, postprandial naps!… Simple philosophy of the contest!… Forty minutes! And he glanced aside and upwards at the phosphorescent cockscomb! Within his mind something said that if he were only suspended up there.…

===Chinese===
得了，那门加农炮除了自己，什么也没通报！它不是任何弹幕的开场；我们自己人也没有砰砰砰地放炮去叫它闭嘴。它只通报了自己的存在，抗议似的说着「**加——农——**」，它的炮弹蹿上极高的天，弹底接住了尚未升起的太阳的反光。一枚发亮的圆盘，像一只飞行中的光环。……美！做勋章图案多美：湛蓝天空里小小的漂亮飞机，穿行在一只只发亮的飞行光环之间！圣徒间的蜻蜓。……不，「与天使和天使长！」……好吧，亲眼见过一回！

加农炮。……对，就该这么叫。像小时候阅兵式上竖着的那些头下脚上、锈迹斑斑的家伙。

不，不是弹幕的信号！好事！不妨说一声「谢天谢地」，因为它们开始得越晚，闹得就越短。……「闹得就越短」这说法头韵难听。「早完早好」才是正经。……没跑儿，八点半、或者八点半整敲着点儿，那帮无聊家伙就要照例上供，多半是齐刷刷正砸在那块地方顶上。……据判断，是三群齐射，每群十二发，群与群间隔半分钟。也许「齐射」这词儿也不对。反正，炮兵统统见鬼去！

那帮家伙图什么？每天早上八点半；每天下午两点半。想来只为表明他们还活着，还照旧无聊。他们有条不紊。那是他们的秘诀。他们无聊的秘诀。想弄死他们，就像想让自由党人闭嘴——那种在不谈政治的俱乐部里偏要大讲党派政治的自由党人……可这营生又非干不可！否则这世界就容不下……噢，饭后打盹！……比试一番的简单哲学！……四十分钟了！他又斜着眼往上去瞟那鸡冠似的磷光！他心里有什么在说：只要把他吊到那上头。……

===Original===
He stepped once more on to the rifle-step and on to the bully-beef-case. He elevated his head cautiously: grey desolation sloped down and away *F.R.R.R.r.r.r!* A gentle purring sound!

He was automatically back, on the duckboard, his breakfast hurting his chest. He said:

“By Jove! I got the fright of my life!” A laugh was called for: he managed it, his whole stomach shaking. And cold!

===Chinese===
他又一次踩上射击踏台、踩上那只咸牛肉箱。小心翼翼地把头抬高：灰色的荒凉坡地一路斜降下去——*呋——尔·尔·尔尔尔！*一阵轻柔的呼噜声！

他整个人自动弹回了木排道上，早饭顶得胸口发疼。他说：

「天哪！我这辈子就没这么吓着过！」这时该笑一笑：他挤出来了，整个肚子直抖。还有那一身冷！

===Original===
A head in a metal pudding-basin—a Suffolk type of blond head, pushed itself from a withdrawn curtain of sacking in the gravel wall beside him, at his back. A voice said with concern:

“There ain’t no beastly snipers, is there, sir. I did ’ope there would’n be henny beastly snipers ’ere. It gives such a beastly lot of extra trouble warning the men.”

Tietjens said it was a beastly skylark that almost walked into his mouth. The Acting Sergeant-Major said with enthusiasm that them ’ere skylarks could fair scare the guts out of you. He remembered a raid in the dark, crawling on ’is ’ands ’n’ knees wen ’e put ’is ’and on a skylark on its nest. Never left ’is nest till ’is ’and was on ’im! Then it went up and fair scared the wind out of ’im. Cor! Never would ’e fergit that!

===Chinese===
一颗戴金属布丁碗的脑袋——萨福克型的金发脑袋——从他背后旁边砾石壕壁上一道撩开的麻袋布帘后探了出来。一个声音关切地说：

「这儿该没那该死的狙击手吧，长官。俺原本就盼着这地界儿别有那劳什子狙击手。有那玩意儿，提醒弟兄们提防，可得多费老鼻子劲儿。」

蒂金斯说，是只该死的云雀，差点儿一头走进他嘴里。代理军士长眉飞色舞地说，那号云雀真能把人魂儿吓出窍。他记得有一回黑夜里出去袭击，用手用膝盖爬着，一伸手正按着窝里一只云雀。手没按到它身上，它死活不离窝！然后它呼一声蹿上天，真把他的气儿都吓没了。好家伙！那事他一辈子忘不了！

===Original===
With an air of carefully pulling parcels out of a carrier’s cart he produced from the cavern behind the sacking two blinking assemblages of tubular khaki-clad limbs. They wavered to erectness, pink cheeses of faces yawning beside tall rifles and bayonets. The Sergeant said:

“Keep yer ’eds down as you go along. You never knows!”

Tietjens told the Lance-Corporal of that party of two that his confounded gas-mask nozzle was broken. Hadn’t he seen that for himself? The dismembered object bobbed on the man’s chest. He was to go and borrow another from another man and see the other drew a new one at once.

===Chinese===
他带着从搬货车里小心卸包裹的那种神情，从麻布帘后的洞窟里弄出两套眨巴着眼、由卡其布管状肢体拼成的人形。他们摇摇晃晃立直了，粉奶酪似的脸在长枪和刺刀旁边打着呵欠。军士说：

「一路上把脑袋都给俺压低了。谁说得准呢！」

蒂金斯对那一组两人里的下士说，他那只见鬼的防毒面具喷嘴坏了。他自己就没瞧见？那件七零八落的东西正在这人胸口一颠一颠。他这就去向别人借一只来，并且盯着对方马上补领一只新的。

===Original===
Tietjens’ eyes were drawn aside and upwards. His knees were still weak. If he were levitated to the level of that thing he would not have to use his legs for support.

The elderly Sergeant went on with enthusiasm about skylarks. Wonderful the trust they showed in hus ’uman beens! Never left ther nesteses till you trod on them tho hall ’ell was rockin’ around them.… An appropriate skylark from above and before the parapet made its shrill and heartless noise heard. No doubt the skylark that Tietjens had frightened—that had frightened him.

Therd bin, the Sergeant went on still enthusiastically, pointing a hand in the direction of the noise, skylarks singin’ on the mornin’ of every straf’e’d ever bin in! Won’erful trust in yumanity! Won’erful hinstinck set in the fethered brest by the Halmighty! For *oo* was goin’ to ’it a skylark on a battlefield!

===Chinese===
蒂金斯的眼睛又被斜着往上一拽。他的膝盖还发软。倘若他凌空浮到那玩意儿的高度，就用不着两条腿支撑了。

上了年纪的军士还在兴头上说云雀。它们对咱们人类那份信任，了不得！哪怕四下里天塌地陷地摇，你不一脚踩上去，它们死也不离窝。……正巧有一只云雀，在胸墙上空、壕前天上，发出那尖利没心肝的叫声。想必就是被蒂金斯吓着的那只——也是吓着了他的那只。

军士仍旧兴致勃勃地接着说，一只手指着声音的方向：俺这辈子挨过的每一回炮击，早上都有云雀在唱！对人类这份信法儿，了不得！全能的上帝安在羽毛胸脯里的天性，了不得！战场上谁*会*去打一只云雀呢！

===Original===
The solitary Man drooped beside his long, bayonetted rifle that was muddied from stock to bayonet attachment. Tietjens said mildly that he thought the Sergeant had got his natural history wrong. He must divide the males from the females. The females sat on the nest through obstinate attachment to their eggs; the males obstinately soared above the nests in order to pour out abuse at other male skylarks in the vicinity.

He said to himself that he must get the doctor to give him a bromide. A filthy state his nerves had got into unknown to himself. The agitation communicated to him by that bird was still turning his stomach round.…

“Gilbert White of Selbourne,” he said to the Sergeant, “called the behaviour of the female *storge*: a good word for it.” But, as for trust in humanity, the Sergeant might take it that larks never gave us a thought. We were part of the landscape and if what destroyed their nests whilst they sat on them was a bit of H.E. shell or the coulter of a plough it was all one to them.

===Chinese===
那个孤单的士兵垂头站在他那杆上了刺刀的长枪旁，枪从枪托到刺刀座全是泥。蒂金斯温和地说，他想军士把博物学弄岔了。得把雄雀跟雌雀分开。雌雀趴在窝里，是对它们的蛋有一种执拗的眷恋；雄雀执拗地飞在窝的上空，为的是冲着附近别的雄云雀骂个痛快。

他心里嘀咕，得让军医给他一剂溴剂。他的神经已经糟到什么田地，自己竟不知道。那只鸟传给他的骚动还在他胃里翻腾。……

「塞尔伯恩的吉尔伯特·怀特，」他对军士说，「管雌雀的这种行为叫 *storge*（慈亲之恋）：这词儿用得好。」至于说信任人类，军士大可这么看：云雀压根儿没把我们放在心上。我们不过是风景的一部分；它们趴在窝里的时候，毁掉它们窝的无论是一块高爆弹（H.E.）弹片还是一张犁铧，对它们全是一回事。

===Original===
The Sergeant said to the rejoined Lance-Corporal whose box now hung correctly on his muddied chest:

“Now it’s **hay** post you gotter wait at!” They were to go along the trench and wait where another trench ran into it and there was a great A in whitewash on a bit of corrugated iron that was half-buried. “You can tell a great **hay** from a bull’s foot as well as another, can’t you Corporal?” patiently.

Wen they Mills bombs come ’e was to send ’is Man into Hay Cumpny dugout fer a fatigue to bring ’em along ’ere, but Hay Cumpny could keep is little lot fer ’isself.

===Chinese===
军士对重新归队的下士说——那人的小箱子此刻端端正正挂在他泥糊糊的胸口：

「这回你该等在 **H** 哨上！」他们要沿堑壕走过去，在另一条壕汇进来的地方等着——半截埋在土里的波纹铁皮上，有个拿白灰刷的大大的 A。「一个大大的 **H** 跟个牛蹄印子，你总还认得出吧，下士？」他耐心十足。

等那些米尔斯手榴弹（Mills bombs）到了，他就把他那个兵派进 **H** 连掩蔽部支个公差，把手榴弹搬到这儿来；不过 H 连自己那点儿货，尽可以他们自己留着。

===Original===
An if they Mills Bombs did’n’ come the Corporal’d better manufacture them on ’is own. An not make no mistakes!

The Lance-Corporal said “Yes sargint, no sargint!” and the two went desultorily wavering along the duckboards, grey silhouettes against the wet bar of light, equilibrating themselves with hands on the walls of the trench.

“Ju ’eer what the orfcer said, Corporal,” the one said to the other. “Wottever’ll ’e say next! Skylarks not trust ’uman beens in battles! Cor!” The other grunted and, mournfully, the voices died out.

===Chinese===
要是米尔斯手榴弹到不了，那下士最好自己动手造它几个。可不许出岔子！

下士应了声「是，军士！不，军士！」两个人便懒懒散散、摇摇晃晃地顺着木排道走了，衬在湿漉漉的一条光带前，是两抹灰色剪影，两只手扶着壕壁找平衡。

「你听听长官说的什么话，下士，」一个对另一个说。「他下一句还不知要说出什么来呢！云雀在打仗的时候不相信人！好家伙！」另一个哼了一声，两个嗓音哀哀地远去了。

===Original===
The cockscomb-shaped splash became of overwhelming interest momentarily to Tietjens; at the same time his mind began upon abstruse calculation of chances. Of his chances! A bad sign when the mind takes to doing that. Chances of direct hits by shells, by rifle bullets, by grenades, by fragments of shells or grenades. By any fragment of metal impinging on soft flesh. He was aware that he was going to be hit in the soft spot behind the collarbone. He was conscious of that spot—the right hand one; he felt none of the rest of his body. It is bad when the mind takes charge like that. A bromide was needed. The doctor must give him one. His mind felt pleasure at the thought of the M.O. A pleasant little fellow of the no account order that knows his job. And carried liquor cheerfully. Confoundedly cheerfully!

He saw the doctor—plainly! It was one of the plainest things he could see of this whole show.… The doctor, a slight figure, vault on to the parapet, like a vaulting horse for height; stand up in the early morning sun.… Blind to the world, but humming “Father O’Flynn.” And stroll in the sunlight, a swagger cane of all things in the world, under his arms, right straight over to the German trench.… Then throw his cap down into that trench. And walk back! Delicately avoiding the strands in the cut apron of wire that he had to walk through!

===Chinese===
那片鸡冠状的白灰一时间对蒂金斯变得趣味无穷；与此同时，他的脑子开始对概率做起玄奥的演算。算他自己的概率！脑子一沾上这个，可不是好兆头。炮弹直接命中的概率，步枪子弹的概率，手榴弹的，炮弹或手榴弹破片的。随便什么金属破片扎进软肉的概率。他知道自己要在锁骨后面那块软窝上挨一下。他能意识到那个点——右边那个；身体其余部分他一概没感觉。脑子这样越俎代庖，糟得很。得来一剂溴剂。军医必须给他一剂。一想到军医官（M.O.），他心里就愉快。一个不起眼类型的小个子好人，本行精通。而且揣着酒，喝得爽快。爽快得没治！

他看见那军医了——清清楚楚！在整个这场大戏里，这是他看得最清楚的事物之一。……军医，一个瘦小身影，一纵身跃上胸墙，那高度跟跳马似的；在清晨的太阳底下站直了。……对全世界视而不见，嘴里却哼着《奥弗林神父》。还在阳光里溜达，胳肢窝底下夹着——天底下什么不好夹——一根轻佻的手杖，径直朝德军堑壕走过去。……然后把军帽往那壕里一扔。再走回来！从他必须穿过的那片剪开的铁丝网裙摆的丝丝缕缕间，灵巧地绕行！

===Original===
The doctor said he had seen a Hun—probably an officer’s batman—cleaning a top-boot with an apron over his knees. The Hun had shied a boot brush at him and he had shied his cap at the Hun. The blinking Hun, he called him! No doubt the fellow had blinked!

No doubt you could do the unthinkable with impunity!

No manner of doubt: if you were blind drunk and all!… And however you strained, in an army you fell into routine. Of a quiet morning you do not expect drunken doctors strolling along your parapet. Besides, the German front lines were very thinly held. Amazingly! There might not have been a Hun with a gun within half a mile of that bootblack!

===Chinese===
军医后来说，他瞧见一个德国佬——多半是个军官的勤务兵——膝盖上围着围裙刷长筒马靴。那德国佬朝他摔过来一把靴刷，他就把军帽朝德国佬摔了过去。他管他叫「那个直眨巴的德国佬」！那家伙八成真是眨巴眼！

毫无疑问：无法无天的事，你可以干了不受罚！

毫无疑义：要是你烂醉如泥，那就更没得说！……再有，任凭你怎么绷着劲儿，在军队里你总会落进套路。消停的早晨，你不会料到有喝醉的军医在你胸墙上游逛。何况德军的前线壕守得极稀。稀得惊人！那个擦靴匠周围半英里之内，未必有一个带枪的德国佬！

===Original===
If he, Tietjens, stood in space, his head level with that cockscomb, he would be in an inviolable vacuum—as far as projectiles were concerned!

He was asking desultorily of the Sergeant whether he often shocked the men by what he said and the Sergeant was answering with blushes: Well, you do *say* things, sir! Not believing in skylarks now! If there was one thing the men believed hit was in the hinstincks of them little creatures!

“So that,” Tietjens said, “they look at me as a sort of an atheist.”

===Chinese===
倘若他，蒂金斯，悬身虚空，头与那鸡冠齐平，他就在一个不可侵犯的真空里——就投射物而言！

他有一搭没一搭地问军士，他说的话常不常把弟兄们吓着，军士红着脸答：长官，您那些话是真*说得出口*！如今连云雀都不信了！弟兄们要是信一样东西，信的就是那些小生灵的天性！

「这么说，」蒂金斯道，「他们就拿我当个无神论者看。」

===Original===
He forced himself to look over the parapet again, climbing heavily to his place of observation. It was sheer impatience and purely culpable technically. But he was in command of the regiment, of an establishment of a thousand and eighteen men, or that used to be the Establishment of a battalion; of a strength of three hundred and thirty three. Say seventy-five per company. And two companies in command of second lieutenants, one just out.… The last four days.… There ought to be, say, eighty pairs of eyes surveying what he was going to survey. If there were fifteen it was as much as there were!… Figures were clean and comforting things. The chance against being struck by a shell-fragment that day, if the Germans came in any force, was fourteen to one against. There were battalions worse off than they. The sixth had only one one six left!

The tortured ground sloped down into mists. Say a quarter of a mile away. The German front lines were just shadows, like the corrugations of photographs of the moon: the paradoses of our own trenches two nights ago! The Germans did not seem to have troubled to chuck up much in the way of parapets. They didn’t. They were coming on. Anyhow they held their front lines always very sparsely.… Was that the phrase? Was it even English?

===Chinese===
他强迫自己再朝胸墙外看，笨重地爬上他的观察位。这纯粹是急性子，技术上讲完全该打板子。可他指挥着这个团——一千零一十八人的编制，或者说，营的编制原本就是这个数——眼下的实力是三百三十三人。合每连七十五人吧。其中两个连还交给少尉们指挥，一个刚出校门。……这四天里。……照理该有八十双眼睛来巡视他就要巡视的地方。有十五双就算顶了天！……数字是干净又安慰人的东西。那天要是德国人大举来攻，挨上一块弹片的赔率是十四比一。还有比他们更惨的营。第六营只剩一百一十六号人了！

饱受蹂躏的地面往下坡沉进雾里。算它四分之一英里吧。德军的前线壕只是些影子，像月亮照片上的环形山脊：两夜之前，那还是我们自己堑壕的背墙（parados）！德国佬似乎懒得堆什么胸墙。他们确实没有。他们正在推进。反正他们的前线壕永远守得很稀。……是这么说的吗？这算英语吗？

===Original===
Above the shadows the mist behaved tortuously: mounting up into umbrella shapes. Like snow-covered umbrella pines.

Disagreeable to force the eye to examine that mist. His stomach turned over.… That was the sacks. A flat, slightly disordered pile of wet sacks, half-right at two hundred yards. No doubt a shell had hit a G.S. wagon coming up with sacks for trenching. Or the bearers had bolted, chucking the sacks down. His eyes had fallen on that scattered pile four times already that morning. Each time his stomach had turned over. The resemblance to prostrate men was appalling. The enemy creeping up.… Christ! Within two hundred yards. So his stomach said. Each time, in spite of the preparation.

Otherwise the ground had been so smashed that it was flat: went down into holes but did not rise up into mounds. That made it look gentle. It sloped down. To the untidiness. They appeared mostly to lie on their faces; Why? Presumably they were mostly Germans pushed back in the last counterattack. Anyhow you saw mostly the seats of their trousers. When you did not, how profound was their repose! You must phrase it a little like that—rhetorically. There was no other way to get the effect of that profoundness. Call it profundity!

===Chinese===
影子上头，雾气曲折变幻：堆成一把把伞的形状。像落满雪的伞松。

逼着自己去细看那片雾，很不舒服。他的胃翻了个个儿。……是那堆麻袋。一摊湿麻袋，平平地、微微散乱地堆在右前方两百码处。想必是一发炮弹击中了一辆往前送土木麻袋的 G.S. 辎重车。再不就是扛袋的撒腿跑了，把麻袋撂下。那天早上，他的目光已经四回落在那摊散堆上。每一回胃都翻了个个儿。那副像趴倒的人的样子，骇人。敌人正爬过来。……老天爷！两百码之内。他的胃这么说。每一回都是，任凭事先怎么有备。

其余的地方，地面已经被砸得平平坦坦：往下陷成坑，却不隆起成包。这使它显得温厚。它往下坡。坡向那片凌乱。他们多半像是趴着的；为什么？想必多半是上次反冲击里被顶回去的德国佬。反正你看到的多半是他们裤子的臀部。看不到臀部的时候，他们的安息何其深沉！你必须稍稍这样措辞——用点修辞。没有别的法子写出那种深沉的效果。就管它叫「深沉」吧！

===Original===
It was different from sleep. Flatter. No doubt when the appalled soul left the weary body, the panting lungs.… Well, you can’t go on with a sentence like that.… But you collapsed inwards. Like the dying pig they sold on trays in the street. Painter fellows doing battlefields never got that *intimate* effect. Intimate to them there. Unknown to the corridors in Whitehall.… Probably because they—the painters—drew from living models or had ideas as to the human form.… But these were not limbs, muscles, torsi.… Collections of tubular shapes in field-grey or mud-colour they were. Chucked about by Almighty God! As if He had dropped them from on high to make them flatten into the earth.… Good gravel soil, that slope and relatively dry. No dew to speak of. The night had been covered.…

Dawn on the battlefield.… Damn it all, why sneer? It *was* dawn on the battlefield.… The trouble was that *this* battle was not over. By no means over. There would be a hundred and eleven years, nine months and twenty-seven days of it still.… No, you could not get the effect of that endless monotony of effort by numbers. Nor yet by saying “Endless monotony of effort”.… It was like bending down to look into darkness of corridors under dark curtains. Under clouds.… Mist.…

At that, with dreadful reluctance his eyes went back to the spectral mists over the photographic shadows. He forced himself to put his glasses on the mists. They mopped and mowed, fantastically; grey, with black shadows; drooping like the dishevelled veils of murdered bodies. They were engaged in fantastic and horrifying layings out of corpses of vast dimensions; in silence but in accord they performed unthinkable tasks. They were the Germans. This was fear. This was the intimate fear of black quiet nights, in dugouts where you heard the obscene suggestions of the miners’ picks below you: tranquil, engrossed. Infinitely threatening.… But not **fear**.

===Chinese===
那跟睡眠不一样。更平。毫无疑问，当惊骇的灵魂离开疲惫的肉身，离开喘息的肺腑。……唉，这样的句子你没法说下去。……可你就是向内塌陷。像街上托盘里卖的那种垂死的猪。画战场油画的家伙们从来抓不到这种*贴肤*的效果。对他们而言贴肤。白厅的走廊里闻所未闻。……多半因为他们——那些画家——照活人模特儿描，或者对人体自有一套想法。……可这些已经不是四肢、肌肉、躯干。……它们是一堆堆管状的东西，原野灰或者烂泥色。被全能的上帝摔得到处都是！仿佛他把他们从半空摔下来，好叫他们摊平进泥土。……好一片砾石土，那道坡，还相当干。没什么露水。夜里天上盖着云。……

战场上的黎明。……见鬼，何必讥诮？这*就是*战场上的黎明。……麻烦在于*这一场*仗还没完。远远没完。往后还有一百一十一年九个月零二十七天。……不，用数字你传达不出那种没完没了的单调劳顿。光说「没完没了的单调劳顿」也不成。……那就像弯下腰，往黑帘幕底下走廊的黑暗里看。云层底下。……雾。……

这样想着，他的眼睛带着十二万分的不情愿，回到照片似的影子上头那片鬼气森森的雾上。他强迫自己把望远镜对准那片雾。它们挤眉弄眼、做着鬼脸，光怪陆离；灰色，带着黑影；垂坠着，像被谋害的尸身上蓬乱的尸衣。它们正在给巨大无朋的尸身入殓，光怪陆离又骇人；它们一声不响却齐心协力，干着不堪设想的活计。它们是德国人。这是恐惧。这是黑色静夜里那种贴肤的恐惧：在掩蔽部里，你听得见脚下矿工镐头淫秽的暗示：安详，专注。威胁无穷。……但不是**恐惧**。

===Original===
It was in effect the desire for privacy. What he dreaded at those normal times when fear visited him at lunch; whilst seeing that the men got their baths or when writing, in a trench, in support, a letter to his bank-manager, was finding himself unhurt, surrounded by figures like the brothers of the Misericordia, going unconcerned about their tasks, noticing him hardly at all.… Whole hillsides, whole stretches of territory, alive with myriads of whitish-grey, long cagoules, with slits for eyeholes. Occasionally one would look at him through the eye-slits in the hoods.… The prisoner!

He would be the prisoner: liable to physical contacts—to being handled and being questioned. An invasion of his privacy!

As a matter of fact that wasn’t so far out; not so dotty as it sounded. If the Huns got him—as they precious near had the night before last!—they would be—they had then been—in gas-masks of various patterns. They must be short of these things: but they looked, certainly, like goblin pigs with sore eyes, the hood with the askew, blind-looking eyeholes and the mouthpiece or the other nose attachment going down into a box, astonishingly like snouts!… Mopping and mowing—no doubt shouting through the masks!

===Chinese===
其实那是对清静的渴望。在恐惧按常例登门的那些时刻——午饭桌上；照看弟兄们洗澡的时候；或者在二线支援壕里给银行经理写信的时候——他怕的是发现自己毫发无伤，却被一群米塞里科迪亚兄弟会（Misericordia）教友似的人形围住，他们淡定地各干各的活，几乎不朝他看一眼。……整面山坡，整片整片的土地，密密麻麻活满了灰白色的连帽长罩袍，罩袍上开着眼缝。偶尔有一个会从兜帽的眼缝里看他一眼。……俘虏！

他就要成为俘虏：任人摆布——被人上下其手、被人盘问。对他清静的侵犯！

其实这念头离谱得也不远；并不像听上去那么疯。要是德国佬抓了他——前天夜里他们差一点儿就得手了！——他们就会是——他们当时也就是——戴着五花八门的防毒面具。这种东西他们准是缺：可他们那模样，实在像害了眼病的小妖猪，兜帽上一双歪斜、瞎子似的眼孔，那衔嘴或者另一个鼻子接口垂进一只小箱子里，活脱脱是猪嘴！……挤眉弄眼——八成还隔着面罩喊叫！

===Original===
They had appeared with startling suddenness and as if with a supernatural silence, beneath a din so overwhelming that you could not any longer bother to notice it. They were there, as it were, under a glass dome of silence that sheltered beneath that dark tumult, in the white illumination of Verey lights that went on. They were there, those of them that had already emerged from holes—astonishingly alert hooded figures with the long rifles that always looked rather amateurish—though, Hell, they weren’t. The hoods and the white light gave them the aspects of Canadian trappers in snow; made them no doubt look still more husky fellows as against our poor rats of Derby men. The heads of goblin pigs were emerging from shell-holes, from rifts in the torn earth, from old trenches.… This ground had been fought over again and again.… Then the counterattack had come through his, Tietjens’, own crowd. One disorderly mob, as you might think, going through a disordered crowd that was damn glad to let them through, realising slowly, in the midst of a general not knowing what was going to happen, that the fellows were reliefs. They shot past you clumsily in a darkness spangled with shafts of light coming from God knows where and appeared going forward, whilst you at least had the satisfaction that, by order, you were going back. In an atmosphere of questioning. What was happening? What was going to happen?… What the bloody hell.… What.…

===Chinese===
他们出现得突兀至极，还仿佛带着一种超自然的寂静，置身在一片震得你再也懒得去留神的喧嚣底下。他们就在那儿，好比说，罩在一只玻璃的静默穹顶之下，那穹顶又栖在那片黑色喧嚣的底下，在一直亮个不停的维利信号弹的白光里。他们就在那儿，那些已经从洞里钻出来的——警醒得出奇的蒙头人影，端着总是显得颇为业余的长枪——虽说，见鬼，他们才不业余。兜帽和白光给了他们雪地里加拿大捕兽人的模样；比起我们那些可怜耗子似的德比兵（Derby men），无疑更衬得他们一伙彪形大汉。小妖猪的脑袋正从弹坑里、从撕裂的土地的缝里、从旧堑壕里往外钻。……这块地皮是反复争夺过的。……然后，反冲击的队伍就从他、蒂金斯自己这伙人当中穿了过去。一支乱糟糟的队伍，你可以这么想，穿过一团巴不得让路的乱糟糟的人，这团人在一种普遍的「不知下一步是什么」当中，慢慢回过味来：那些人是来接防的。他们在一片缀满光柱的黑暗里——光柱不知打哪儿来——笨手笨脚地从你身边冲过去，人影显出往前去的架势，而你至少还满意地知道：奉命后退的是你。在一种人人发问的空气里。正在发生什么？将要发生什么？……到底他娘的搞什么。……什么。……

===Original===
Tidy-sized shells began to drop among them saying: “Wee… ee… ry.… Whack!” Some fellow showed Tietjens the way through an immense apron of wire that was beginning to fly about. He, Tietjens, was carrying a hell of a lot of paper folders and books. They ought to have evacuated an hour ago; or the Huns ought not to have got out of their holes for an hour.… But the Colonel had been too… too exalted. Call it too exalted. He was not going to evacuate for a pack of… Damn orders!… The fellow, Mckechnie, had at last had to beg Tietjens to give the order.… Not that the order mattered. The men could not have held ten minutes longer. The ghostly Huns would have been in the trenches. But the Company Commanders knew that there was a Divisional Order to retire, and no doubt they had passed it on to their subalterns before getting killed. Still, that Bn. H.Q. should have given the order made it better even if there was no one to take it to the companies. It turned a practical expulsion into an officially strategic retreat.… And damn good divisonal staff work at that. They had been fitted into beautiful, clean, new trenches, all ready for them—like chessmen fitting into their boxes. Damn good for a beaten army that was being forced off the face of the earth. Into the English Channel.… What made them stick it? What the devil made the men stick it? They were unbelievable.

===Chinese===
大小正合适的炮弹开始落在他们中间，说着：「唿……咿……噜。……咣！」有什么人给蒂金斯指点路，穿过一片已经开始四处乱飞的巨型铁丝网裙。他，蒂金斯，怀里抱着要命的一大摞文件夹和书。他们本该一小时前就撤了；要不然，德国佬本该一小时后再从洞里出来。……可上校太……太高蹈了。就说高蹈吧。他才不肯为一帮子……撤呢。命令见鬼去！……那个家伙，麦克凯克尼，最后只好求蒂金斯来下这道命令。……倒不是这道命令管什么用。弟兄们本来也撑不过十分钟了。鬼影似的德国佬就要进壕了。可各连连长知道有师部的撤退令，他们无疑在阵亡之前已经传给了各自的排级军官。尽管如此，营部（Bn. H.Q.）下了这道令，事情就好看了些，哪怕已经没人能把令送到各连。它把一场实际上的驱逐，变成了官方认定的战略撤退。……而且师部的参谋业务还真他娘的漂亮。他们被装进了一条条漂亮、干净、全新的堑壕，一切现成——像棋子各归各的格。对一支正被人从地皮上往下抹的败军来说，真他娘的不赖。往英吉利海峡里抹。……是什么撑着他们扛下来？到底是什么鬼东西撑着弟兄们扛下来？难以置信。

===Original===
There was a stroking on his leg. A gentle, timid stroking! Well, he *ought* to get down: it was setting a bad example. The admirable trenches were perfectly efficiently fitted up with spy-holes. For himself he always disliked them. You thought of a rifle bullet coming smack through them and guided by the telescope into your right eye. Or perhaps you would not have a telescope. Anyhow you wouldn’t know.…

There were still the three wheels, atilt, attached to slanting axles: in a haze of disintegrated wire, that, bedewed, made profuse patterns like frost on a window. There was their own apron—a perfect village!—of wire over which he looked. Fairly intact. The Germans had put up some of their own in front of the lost trenches, a quarter of a mile off: over the reposing untidinesses. In between there was a perfect maze: their own of the night before last. How the deuce had it not been *all* mashed to pieces by the last Hun barrage? Yet there were three frosty erections—like fairy sheds, halfway between the two lines. And, suspended in them, as there would have to be, three bundles of rags and what appeared to be a very large, squashed crow. How the devil had that fellow managed to get smashed into that shape? It was improbable. There was also—suspended, too, a tall melodramatic object, the head cast back to the sky. One arm raised in the attitude of, say, a Walter Scott Highland officer waving his men on. Waving a sword that wasn’t there.… That was what wire did for you. Supported you in grotesque attitudes, even in death! The beastly stuff! The men said that was Lieutenant Constantine. It might well be. The night before last he, Tietjens, had looked round at all the officers that were in H.Q. dugout, come for a last moment conference. He had speculated on which of them would be killed. Ghostly! Well, they had all been killed: and more on to that. But his premonition hadn’t run to thinking that Constantine would get caught up in the wire. But perhaps it was not Constantine. Probably they would never know. The Huns would be where he stood by lunchtime. If the attack of which Brigade H.Q. had warned them came off. But it mightn’t.…

===Chinese===
他的腿上有一阵轻抚。一阵轻轻的、怯生生的抚摸！唉，他*是*该下来了：这是带坏样子。这几条令人赞叹的堑壕，早就一五一十装备好了窥孔。他自己一向讨厌那玩意儿。你会想到一颗步枪子弹不偏不倚穿透它，顺着望远镜导进你的右眼。也许你还没望远镜。反正你无从知道。……

三只轮子还歪插在斜轴上，罩在一蓬碎散铁丝的迷蒙里，那铁丝凝着露珠，织出繁复的花样，像窗上的霜。那是他们自己的铁丝裙——好一座齐全的小村！——他正从它上面望出去。大体完好。德国佬在他们丢掉的堑壕前面、四分之一英里外，也竖起了一些自己的：罩着那些安息的凌乱。两线之间是一整座迷宫：他们自己前夜布的。鬼知道它怎么就没被德国佬最后那通弹幕*整个*犁碎？居然还立着三座挂霜的架子——像童话里的小棚屋，正卡在两线之间半道上。而在它们里面，必然挂着地，吊着三捆破布，还有一只看模样硕大无比、被拍扁的乌鸦。那家伙到底怎么把自己砸成那副形状的？说不通。还有——也吊着——一个高挑的、闹剧腔的人形，头朝后仰面朝天。一条胳膊举着，那姿势，活像沃尔特·司各特笔下的苏格兰军官在挥兵向前。挥着一柄并不存在的剑。……这就是铁丝给你干的好事。把你架在最狰狞的姿势里，死了也不放过！这劳什子玩意儿！弟兄们说那是康斯坦丁中尉。很可能就是。前天夜里，他，蒂金斯，环视过掩蔽部里到齐了开最后关头会议的全体军官。他还揣度过他们中间谁会死。邪门！结果他们全死了：还不止。可他的预感没料到康斯坦丁会挂进铁丝网里。但也可能不是康斯坦丁。多半永远无从知道。到午饭时分，德国佬就会站到他此刻站的地上。如果旅部预警的那场进攻当真来了的话。但也可能不来。……
